# scripts/orchestrator

## hcom bus: `hcom-dispatch` and `bus-history.sh`

Plan 025 W2b (C-1). The omp seats talk over one private hcom store per program, selected by `HCOM_DIR` (hcom 0.7.30 and 0.7.31). Both scripts read the store from `HCOM_DIR` when it is set, else derive it from the checkout they run in (`regime-env`, U33: `~/.local/state/hcom/<slug>`, section "regime-init and regime-env"), and refuse to run when that store's `hcom.db` is absent, because `hcom events` creates a store on first use and only the seats' launch may do that. Every test runs against a scratch store, never `~/.hcom`.

### Routes

| Direction                                            | Command                                                              | hcom call                                                                     |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| orchestrator to a seat (TASK, VERDICT, MERGED, HOLD) | `hcom-dispatch <team> <from> <to> <line...>`                         | `hcom send @<to> --thread <team> --from <from> --json -- <line>`              |
| seat to the orchestrator (RESULT, PONG)              | the seat's own `hcom send --thread <team> -- '<line>'`, no target    | the thread the TASK seeded; an external sender is not addressable with `@`    |
| records                                              | `bus-history.sh <team> [--limit <n>] [--since <ts>] [--kind <kind>]` | `hcom events --type message --thread <team> --full --last <n> [--after <ts>]` |

`<to>` is the seat's exact hcom name, so a seat hcom does not know fails with hcom's own `Error: No active agent for @<to>` (exit 1, stderr). hcom accepts any thread name for a mention send and `hcom start` sets no tag (`src/commands/start.rs:486`), so the team of a seat is not checked here; a malformed team is refused by the grammar `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`. The line's first word must be a kind (`^[A-Z]+$`), so the retired agmsg-dispatch form with a pane argument (`<to> - "TASK ..."`) is refused with the usage line before any hcom call; hcom delivers to a bound seat itself. The audit is no seat and has no bus route: each audit is one `pr-audit.sh <pr>` run whose verdict the orchestrator reads from the file it prints (U119 round 3; `omp-run.sh --role audit` before it, U57); the audit seat's `hcom-reply` lever was removed with the seat (U58).

### Line kinds

A bus line's first word is its kind, and the levers treat it as an opaque word (`bus-history.sh --kind` matches it exactly):

| Kind      | Sent by          | Carries                                                                                                  |
| --------- | ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `TASK`    | the orchestrator | `task_id`, `brief=`, `branch=`; a fix unit's `findings=`                                                 |
| `RESULT`  | a worker seat    | `task_id`, `status=`, `pr=`, `head=`                                                                     |
| `VERDICT` | the orchestrator | `task_id`, `verdict=` (`correct` or `incorrect`; a `blocked` audit run sends none), `head=`, `findings=` |
| `PONG`    | any seat         | `task_id`, `status=` (below) and a one-sentence `note=`                                                  |
| `MERGED`  | the orchestrator | `task_id`, `sha=` of the merge commit                                                                    |
| `HOLD`    | the orchestrator | `task_id`, `note=` why the task waits                                                                    |

The kinds carry no prefix (U35). A first word outside the table is no kind: the stop gate moves no task on it, and `hcom-dispatch` refuses one that is not `^[A-Z]+$` (the retired `AGMSG-TASK`, for one) but sends any other upper-case word as written, so the sender keeps to the table. A worker's `PONG` carries `status=blocked` or `status=question` (the `hcom-worker` skill, "Blocked"), and no `PONG` moves a task in the stop gate. The audit seat's PONG statuses (`status=findings` and the measurement requests `measure-request`, `measured`, `measure-refused`) left with the seat (U58): a runtime claim an audit run cannot check is one of its findings, which the orchestrator reproduces in a `git archive` copy of the head (the `hcom-orchestration` skill, step 4).

### Receipt and history

`hcom-dispatch` prints one line, `id=<event id> at=<hcom ts> to=<to> kind=<first word>`, read back from the bus by exact id, so the row exists before the receipt does. `bus-history.sh` prints `id<TAB>at<TAB>from<TAB>to<TAB>kind<TAB>body`, ascending by id; `to` is hcom's `delivered_to` joined with commas (`-` when empty, as for a seat's thread reply) and `body` escapes backslash, tab, CR and LF as `\\`, `\t`, `\r` and `\n`, so a row is one line of five tabs.

### hcom facts the scripts rest on (0.7.30, re-measured on 0.7.31)

| Flag or behaviour                                                                   | Source                                                    |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| `send @name -- text`, "Everything after -- is the message", `@name` exact base name | `hcom send --help`                                        |
| `--from <name>` external sender identity; the row's `from`                          | `src/commands/send.rs:96`, `:403`                         |
| `--json` receipt `{"delivered_to": [...], "event_id": N}` on stdout                 | `send.rs:1135`; observed                                  |
| `--thread <name>` routing; a thread without members is refused                      | `send.rs:300`                                             |
| `events --type message --thread --last --after --full --sql`                        | `hcom events --help`; `src/commands/events.rs:33, 39, 45` |
| `--last` is `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT n`                                              | `events.rs:185`                                           |
| `HCOM_DIR` selects the store; `hcom.db` inside it                                   | `hcom start --help`; observed                             |
| an unknown `@name` fails with `Error: No active agent for @name`, exit 1            | observed                                                  |
| `hcom events` creates an absent store                                               | observed                                                  |

Observed means run against hcom 0.7.30 and 0.7.31 in a scratch store, on 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24 (regime-cutover U22, PR #511, facts 1 to 13 identical); the source lines above are at tag `v0.7.30`. `tests/fixtures/hcom/events-u5team.jsonl` holds the captured rows and `tests/fixtures/hcom/fake-hcom.py` replays the dialect in CI, where hcom is absent; `tests/unit/test_hcom_dispatch.py` also runs the real binary in a private store when one is on PATH.

Three 0.7.31 observations from the live pilot on this host (regime-cutover U22b), which `regime-start` and `regime-stop` rest on:

- `hcom kill <name>` and `hcom list --stopped <name>` accept the seat's `base_name` word (`rosa`) or its full name (`worker-2-rosa`), not its tag (`worker-2`), while alive seats list as `<tag>-<word>` (ledger and decisions 2026-10-24T16:58:00Z).
- The named stopped form prints a `Stopped:` key/value block, the `Resume:` hint, then `Stop history (<n> events):` with `(latest)` on the newest event, which is the line `regime-start` parses (ledger and decisions 2026-10-24T16:58:00Z).
- `hcom list --format` with `name`, `base_name`, `tag` and `status` works as `regime-start` uses it (ledger 2026-10-24T16:58:00Z).

## `omp-run.sh`: one non-interactive omp turn per role

Plan 025 W6a (U8). The orchestrator seat, and the coordinator today, runs a headless omp turn in a seat role with one command. The script composes `omp --profile <role> --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/<role>.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/<role>.yml -e <dir>... --no-session --max-time <s> [--model <provider/id>] --mode <text|json> -p "<prompt file contents>"` (omp 18.11.3; `omp --help` lists every flag), runs it in `--cwd`, and fails closed: an unknown role, an absent or empty prompt file, an absent policy or profile overlay, extension directory or cwd is exit 2 with the path, before omp starts. The two overlays are the ones `hcom-seat` passes every role ("`hcom-seat`" below): the profile overlay carries the manifest's `modelRoles.default` for the role, which a named profile reads nowhere else. omp's stderr is held and replayed after the run; a `Failed to load extension <path>: <error>` line in it is exit 2 too, with the path in the refusal (U1 report F5). Otherwise omp's own exit status is returned. HOME is the caller's, so the seats run as the operator, and `--approval-mode` is never passed: the role's overlay owns it (`home/dot_agents/omp/policies/README.md`).

Since U66 the script passes `--no-extensions` first, for every role: the `-e` directories still load, and no ambient extension module does (the cwd's `.omp/extensions`, `.omp/hooks`, `.omp/plugins`, a settings `extensions` entry, the profile's plugin store; measured under a scratch HOME with omp 18.11.3, where a planted `.omp/extensions/probe.ts`, `.omp/hooks/pre/probe-hook.ts`, `.omp/plugins` package and `extensions:` entry all loaded without the flag and none with it, while the bridge, the SIWC extension and the stop gate still loaded). It is a pinned lever: the generator installs it as `~/.local/bin/common/omp-run.sh` with a `claude.levers.sha256` pin, it refuses to run when its own bytes differ from the pin (the U31 self-check), and the SKILL, `AGENTS.md` and the regime template run that installed copy, so every repository runs the same reviewed bytes. omp is the first `omp` on `PATH` whose physical directory is outside every `.sandbox` directory: inside a seat, `PATH` starts with the seat shim `<seats-root>/.sandbox/<name>/bin/omp` (U85), which is not the omp package and has no discovery registry beside it.

| Role           | Profile and overlays                                                                                                        | Approval, from the overlay                                                                                          | Used for                                      |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| `orchestrator` | `--profile orchestrator --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/orchestrator.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/orchestrator.yml`  | `yolo`, merge and push rules in `bash.patterns`                                                                     | tasking, acceptance, the audited squash merge |
| `worker`       | `--profile worker --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/worker.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/worker.yml`                    | `yolo`, every push to `main` denied                                                                                 | implementing a unit in its worktree           |
| `audit`        | `--profile audit --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/audit.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/audit.yml`, plus the third below | `always-ask` with the write tools denied; a denied call returns omp's "requires approval" text to the model (#G1-2) | the read-only task-level audit of a head      |

`--role audit` is the audit path (`hcom-orchestration` SKILL, step 4): one run per head, fresh by `--no-session`, never a long-lived seat that carries earlier audits in its context. Its `--cwd` is a worktree at the head, read as data only. The rules are the whole `AGENTS.md` of `refs/remotes/origin/main` in that repository (`git show`; the full ref, so a tag or a local branch named `origin/main` cannot stand in for it), put before the caller's prompt between `<base-rules>` tags, so a pull request cannot rewrite its own auditor's rules; Claude Code Review likewise reads its instructions "from the base or default branch and never from the pull request's own changes" (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review). omp reads project context (context files such as `AGENTS.md`, rules such as `.clinerules` and `.agent/rules`, skills, settings, hooks, tools, MCP servers) from its cwd and the cwd's ancestors through the discovery providers of its capability registry (omp 18.11.3 `src/discovery/index.ts`), and its `disabledProviders` setting switches a provider off for user and project scope alike (`src/capability/index.ts` `filterProviders`). So omp never runs in the head, and runs with every provider off: the script reads the registry's provider ids with `bun` from the `src/discovery/index.ts` of the package `omp` resolves to (`getAllProvidersInfo`; omp itself runs on bun), adds the entries the two overlays already disable (a later overlay's list replaces an earlier one), and passes that `disabledProviders` as a third `--config`. A name list of context files would miss the next provider; the registry cannot. bun is the PATH entry made absolute, never dereferenced: mise's bun shim is a symlink to the mise executable, which acts as bun only under that name and rejects `--no-install` under its own. The model, the policy and the `-e` extensions do not come from discovery and stay. One more reader sits outside the providers: the task tool's agent list (`src/task/discovery.ts`, every `.omp/agents/*.md` in the cwd's ancestors), which `TaskTool.create` puts into the task tool's description. So the audit also passes `--tools=read,grep,glob,bash`, the read tools `audit.yml` lets run and no tool it denies: `resolveBuiltinToolPlan` (`src/tools/index.ts`) builds only the listed tools, so the task tool, and with it the agent scan, never exists in the run. omp runs in `<scratch>/cwd`, an empty directory, `<scratch>` made directly in `TMPDIR` (default `/tmp`; made absolute with `pwd -P` and exported so, so the exit trap removes the right directory after the `cd` and omp's `os.tmpdir()` is absolute), with `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` naming the worktree; omp's plugin registry walk, which no provider gates, stops before `TMPDIR` (`src/discovery/helpers.ts` `getProjectDiscoveryCeilings`). `<scratch>` is removed on exit, a failed `realpath` of it included. A cwd outside git, an absent `refs/remotes/origin/main`, no `AGENTS.md` there, bun absent, no registry beside omp, no provider list from it or a `TMPDIR` that cannot be made absolute are exit 2 before omp starts.

Measured 2026-10-25 with the real omp 18.11.3 (no model call: a shim ran omp-run.sh's exact argv with `--mode rpc` and one `get_state` in place of `--mode json -p`, and read the assembled `systemPrompt`): with `AGENTS.md`, an `alwaysApply` `.clinerules`, an `alwaysApply` `.agent/rules/planted.md`, a `.omp/config.yml` and a `.claude/settings.json` (both `disabledProviders: []`) in the parent of `TMPDIR`, the run without the third overlay loaded all three planted texts and the run with it loaded none; both resolved the model `openai-siwc/gpt-6-astra` from the audit profile overlay through the SIWC extension. The registry held 22 providers, all switched off.

Measured 2026-10-25 the same way (U57d), with mise's shims first on `PATH` (`bun` the shim, a symlink to `~/.local/bin/mise`) and a `.omp/agents/planted.md` (`description: PLANTED-AGENT-MARKER …`) in the parent of `TMPDIR`: without `--tools` the tools were `read, bash, edit, eval, glob, grep, task, wait, todo, web_search, write` and the task tool's schema held the planted description; with it the tools were `read, grep, glob, bash` and neither the `systemPrompt` nor any tool schema held `planted`. Both runs resolved `gpt-6-astra` and exited 0 with an empty stderr; the 9af9c02f script refused the same call with `cannot list omp's discovery providers`, mise having rejected `--no-install`.

`--mode text` (default) passes omp's own print-mode text through and `--mode json` passes its event stream through unchanged, one JSON object per line (`tool_execution_start`, `tool_execution_end`, `turn_end`, `agent_end`; measured in `.orchestration/validation/025-pilot-facts.md` #G1-3). Nothing is parsed, so a caller that wants only the final text reads the last `turn_end` of the JSON stream itself. Extensions default to the bridge `~/.local/lib/omp-extensions/pi-claude-bridge` and the SIWC `~/.agents/omp/extensions/openai-siwc`; `--ext <dir>` (repeatable) replaces that list. `--max-time` defaults to 600 seconds. omp reads a prompt that starts with `-` as a flag, so start a prompt file with a word or a heading.

```bash
# An omp run under the audit profile (the audit route until U119 round 3; the audit is `pr-audit.sh`, below): base rules from origin/main, the final text (ending in its Verdict line) to a file, 30-minute bound.
~/.local/bin/common/omp-run.sh --role audit --prompt-file audit-U8.md --cwd /path/to/worktree-at-head --max-time 1800 > verdicts/U8-<head>.md

# The worker seat runs a task with a model override and the bridge staged elsewhere.
~/.local/bin/common/omp-run.sh --role worker --prompt-file task.md --cwd /path/to/worktree --model claude-bridge/claude-haiku-4-5 --ext /path/to/pi-claude-bridge
```

`tests/unit/test_omp_run.py` runs the script against `tests/fixtures/omp-run/fake-omp.py`, copied to `<package>/dist/cli.js` beside the fixture registry `tests/fixtures/omp-run/discovery-index.ts` as `<package>/src/discovery/index.ts`, and `tests/fixtures/omp-run/fake-bun.py`. The fake omp records argv, cwd, HOME, the run's `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and `TMPDIR`, the names in its cwd and the text of each `--config` overlay and replays `tests/fixtures/omp-run/stream.jsonl`; the fake bun records its argv and cwd and prints a canned provider list. One test runs the real bun, when installed, on the fixture registry. The real omp is never started by the tests.

### Threat table

The script starts a seat that acts under its role's policy on a caller-supplied prompt in a caller-supplied worktree, so it is trust-boundary code. Each test name is in `tests/unit/test_omp_run.py`.

| Control                                                   | Disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `omp-run.sh`, the composed command                        | closed: `test_the_audit_argv_is_composed_field_by_field_and_the_json_stream_passes_through` (exact argv and overlay texts, `--approval-mode` absent) and `test_text_mode_the_timeout_and_the_two_extension_directories_are_the_defaults`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `omp-run.sh`, the refusals                                | closed: `test_missing_inputs_are_refused_before_omp_runs` (role, mode, prompt, policy and profile overlay, extension directory, cwd, omp, bun, registry and provider list absent, a `TMPDIR` that does not resolve; nothing recorded, no scratch directory left) and `test_a_failed_extension_load_is_a_refusal_naming_the_path`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| the `omp` executable                                      | closed: the first `omp` in a `PATH` directory outside every `.sandbox` directory, resolved with `pwd -P` and `realpath` before the `cd`, so a relative PATH entry cannot pick `<cwd>/bin/omp` from a PR head and a seat's shim is never taken for the package; `test_omp_is_resolved_before_the_chdir_so_a_worktree_bin_cannot_replace_it`, `test_a_seat_shim_first_on_path_is_skipped_for_the_real_omp` (U66)                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ambient extension modules                                 | closed: `--no-extensions` is the first argument for every role (`test_the_audit_argv_is_composed_field_by_field_and_the_json_stream_passes_through`, `test_text_mode_the_timeout_and_the_two_extension_directories_are_the_defaults`, U66)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| the script's own bytes                                    | closed: installed as `~/.local/bin/common/omp-run.sh` with its pin and self-check (`tests/unit/test_lever_self_check.py`, `test_the_levers_install_byte_identical_and_pinned`); residual: the copy is mode 0755 and its owner can rewrite it and the pin, so the check detects a rewrite and does not prevent one; owner: U85 (containment)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| the policy and profile overlays                           | closed: both files must exist here, as in `hcom-seat`, and omp's `--config` is strict (`home/dot_agents/omp/policies/README.md`); the `missing policy overlay` and `missing profile overlay` cases of the refusal test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| the extension directories                                 | closed: each must exist, relative paths are resolved with `realpath` before the `cd` (`test_a_relative_extension_path_is_made_absolute_before_the_chdir`), and a `Failed to load extension` line is exit 2                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| the prompt file                                           | residual: the prompt reaches the model whole; what its tool calls may do is bounded by the role's overlay and omp's approval gate, not here; owner: the caller that writes the prompt                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `--role audit`, the rules                                 | closed: `test_the_audit_argv_is_composed_field_by_field_and_the_json_stream_passes_through` (the prompt holds `refs/remotes/origin/main`'s rule, not the head's) and `test_the_audit_rules_come_from_origin_main_when_the_head_worktree_is_dirty`; the `audit cwd outside git`, `no origin/main`, `origin/main only a tag and a branch` and `no AGENTS.md at origin/main` cases of the refusal test                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `--role audit`, the head as data                          | closed: `test_the_audit_runs_in_an_empty_scratch_directory_with_git_reading_the_head` (an empty cwd under `TMPDIR`, git reads the head, the directory is gone afterwards), `test_a_scratch_directory_realpath_cannot_resolve_is_still_removed`, `test_a_relative_tmpdir_is_made_absolute_so_the_scratch_directory_is_removed_after_the_chdir` and the 2026-10-25 real-omp measurement above; residual: the head's files still reach the model as tool output, labelled data in the prompt and in the rules (OWASP LLM01:2025, segregate external content); owner: the `AGENTS.md` "Audit" rules |
| `--role audit`, project context above the scratch         | closed: every provider of omp's registry is off (`test_project_context_above_the_scratch_directory_is_switched_off_not_refused`: an `AGENTS.md`, `.clinerules`, `.agent` and `.git` above the scratch, the provider overlay last; `test_the_real_bun_disables_every_registered_provider_and_keeps_the_overlay_entries`), the task tool and its `.omp/agents` scan are not built (`test_the_audit_registers_only_the_read_tools_so_no_task_tool_lists_agents`), and both real-omp measurements above                                                                                             |
| `--role audit`, bun behind a mise shim                    | closed: bun is called by its PATH name, its directory made absolute; `test_bun_is_called_through_its_mise_shim_not_the_mise_executable_behind_it` and the U57d real-omp measurement above                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `--cwd` and the inherited HOME                            | residual: omp runs in the named worktree (for `--role audit`, the scratch directory) as the operator, reading the operator's `~/.agents/omp/policies` and `~/.agents/omp/profiles`; the account and the worktree stay the boundary (U6, "approval is not containment"); owner: the seat policies                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| the orchestrator effect: a `yolo` seat acts on the prompt | residual: the orchestrator and worker overlays deny the merge, push and publish commands (`home/dot_agents/omp/policies/README.md`); `--approval-mode` is never widened here, asserted by the exact argv test; owner: the seat policies                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `tests/unit/test_omp_run.py`                              | residual: runs only the fake under a scratch HOME, so a divergence from the real omp shows up in a live seat; owner: the first seat run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `tests/fixtures/omp-run/fake-omp.py`, `stream.jsonl`      | residual: the stream's event names follow the measured #G1-3 run, not a verbatim capture; owner: the first seat run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `README.md`                                               | residual: prose; owner: this directory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## `ci-rerun.sh`: one rerun of a known CI flake

Plan 024 P1-3 (T133). `scripts/orchestrator/ci-rerun.sh <run-id>` reruns a workflow run's failed jobs once, and only when every failed, timed-out or cancelled job's log matches a signature in `.github/flakes.yaml` and the run's head is still its pull request's head; the script's shdoc lists every check and exit code. Owner: the orchestrator seat (`hcom-orchestration` SKILL, step 3, "CI is green"), or the operator, never a worker seat: it acts on a CI result, not on a unit's code. It runs from the main checkout by its checkout path, not as a pinned lever under `~/.local/bin/common`, because its records are that checkout's `.orchestration/validation/ci-reruns.tsv` and the lock directory `ci-reruns.lock.d/<run-id>` beside it (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` from the script's own directory), so a run is rerun at most once per checkout. Its only GitHub write is `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed`, which no seat policy denies. `tests/unit/test_ci_rerun.py` covers it. Residual: the checkout copy is not sha256-pinned, so a rewritten checkout script runs as written; the reviewed copy reaches `main` only through the audited merge; owner: the operator who runs it from a checkout at `main`.

## `brief-check.py`: a brief names its closing mechanism before the TASK

Regime-cutover U104 (handoff R1) and U121 (standing order 37). `~/.local/bin/common/brief-check.py .orchestration/orchestrate/<program>/briefs/U<n>-<slug>.md`, run from the checkout, runs between a written brief and its TASK line (`hcom-orchestration` SKILL, step 1). A brief is a sequence of upper-case section headings, each a line that is exactly its name (`GOAL`, `SCOPE`, `CHANGE`, `CONTEXT`, `ACCEPTANCE`, `VERIFY`, `TIMEBOX`, `FORBIDDEN`, `CLOSURE`, `TRUST`, `REPORT`, `STANDING`, plus the `WORKER RULES (...)` line), each followed by its text; the script parses it into one `dict[str, str]`, so a term in SCOPE or FORBIDDEN fires nothing. Exit 0, printing nothing: the GOAL, CHANGE and CONTEXT sections hold none of the trigger terms in the script's one tuple (shell, tokeniz*, parse, parser, regex, hook, worktree, config, path, process, signal, supervisor, curl, wget, heredoc, quote, bound, refuse, fail closed, untrusted, sandbox, lint, rule, allowlist, denylist, walk, census; whole words, any case, a backticked term included), or the `CLOSURE` section is one line naming one of the four forms, `ast`, `parser-module`, `allowlist` or `remove`, optionally followed by a colon and free text, and the `TRUST` section is at least 40 characters and holds the word `trusted` or `trusts`. Exit 1, one reason per line on stdout: a term fired and there is no `CLOSURE`, or `CLOSURE` is empty, names two forms or holds any other line; a term fired and there is no `TRUST`, or it is too short, or it names nothing as trusted; `CLOSURE` names `allowlist` or `ast` while CHANGE holds `denylist`, `forbidden words` or `list of commands` (a denylist dressed as a form); or the brief has no GOAL, CHANGE or CONTEXT section. Exit 2 on usage, a file that cannot be read, or a heading that appears twice (named with both line numbers; merging the bodies would hide an empty second `CLOSURE`), never read as clean. U130 (plan P-41) adds one more exit-1 line, `SCOPE names <n> levers (<names>); split into sequenced units of at most two levers each (plan P-41: multi-lever briefs took 6.5 audits, single-surface ones 2)`, when the brief's SCOPE names more than two levers in backticked paths: the lever set is the `claude.levers.sha256` names of the manifest the self-check reads, by indentation alone and in the standard library (U136): the top-level `claude:` mapping, its `levers:` key, its `sha256:` key, then the `<name>: <64 hex>` pin lines, every pin at the one indentation the first fixes (any depth beyond `sha256:`) and the self pin the list's own `brief-check.py` entry read by that rule, with blank and `#` comment lines skipped and the list ending at the first line indented less than its entries; a `levers:` block under another key is not the list, a pin line of another shape is exit 2 naming the line, and a manifest without the block is exit 2 before the self pin is looked for, so the list is spelled once (no YAML beyond that one mapping path: no anchors, no flow style, no quoted keys), a path counts by its basename or as `executable_<name>` under `home/dot_local/bin/common/` (the generated copy counts once with its source), and a path under `tests/` or named `README.md`, `generated-agent-outputs.json` or `agent-config.yaml` never counts; the count and the threshold are the whole rule, so a unit may still touch any number of files that are not levers. It is installed and pinned like the levers below (`claude.levers.sha256`; the same self-check, in Python with `hashlib`, exits 2 with nothing on stdout when the copy differs from its pin) and allowed by `orchestrator.yml` at its installed path, so a repository without `scripts/orchestrator/` runs the same checked copy. The thirteen treadmill rows behind the rule are in the program's `handoff/02-audit-treadmill-analysis.md` (U109 and U112 added the trust boundary); `tests/unit/test_brief_check.py` runs the checkout script as a subprocess under `python -I` and the installed copy under a scratch HOME as the SKILL spells it. The lever call that refuses a program PR whose brief fails this check is a follow-up after U120. U249 (standing order 44) adds the ACCEPTANCE rule: when SCOPE names a repository path (a relative backticked span of two or more names, or one file name with an extension; `~/...`, `/tmp/...`, `../...` and a bare word are not), the `ACCEPTANCE` section must exist and name one gate of each of two classes, lint (`scripts/ci/run-on-lint-files.sh`, `make format`, the Makefile target CI's test job runs before the unit tests) and test (`python -m unittest`, `make unit-test`, `node --test`, or, since U280, a records brief's own check script run as `python3 -I <path>/check-<name>.py`, the gate that proves a unit changing no repository file), each missing class one exit-1 line (`ACCEPTANCE names no lint gate (...)`, `ACCEPTANCE names no test gate (...)`, or `no ACCEPTANCE section`), so a sweep brief whose SCOPE writes under `~/` alone is exempt by its shape and the check judges the gates' names, never whether they were run (the body's pasted lines).

## `ledger-row.py`: the coordinator's one writer of a ledger row

Regime-cutover U222. Every `ledger.tsv` row the coordinator wrote was composed by hand in a Python cell, and four of them were wrong in ways the readers found later: a merge row whose verifier cell carried a note hid three merges from `rounds-check.py`, which reads a merge row as one whose verifier is exactly `pr-merge.sh` (decisions 2026-11-06T00:18:19Z); a `-` receipt row written against an audited head became the newest row of that head and voided every later carry from it, since `pr-merge.sh` reads the newest row of the earlier head whatever its verdict (2026-11-05T23:53:08Z); a verdict file stored under a fix unit's name while the row named the parent unit refused the next audit (2026-11-07T12:39:21Z); a carried row whose digest ended with a semicolon failed the merge gate's whole-word regex (2026-11-04T07:04:58Z). `python3 -I -S ~/.local/bin/common/ledger-row.py <store> <kind> ...` composes one frozen `Row` of the reader's header from named arguments and the clock (`ts` is now in UTC under `TS_FORMAT`, never an argument), with the verdict and the verifier's fixed words chosen by the kind and never typed by the caller, in six kinds: `merged` (verdict `-`, verifier exactly `pr-merge.sh`, evidence the `merged pr=<pr> head=<sha> commit=<40 hex>` receipt whose pr and head must equal the arguments, then `; <note>`), `carried` (verdict `carried`, verifier `audit-carry.py (<note>)`, evidence the digest lever's line, which must hold `carried <earlier> <head> <sha256>` as the gate's regex reads it with the second sha the head, then `; <evidence note>`, so the digest is a whole word before a separator), `sweep` (pr `-`, verdict `sweep`, verifier `security-audit-skill <pin8> (full audit mode on the srt worker seat <seat>, run-<n>; TASK U<n>)` with `<pin8>` the first 8 characters of the manifest's `assets.security-audit-skill.pin`, `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml` else the checkout's, read as `pr-merge.sh`'s `sweep_pin_probe` reads it, evidence `<findings>; pr <pr or none>; <count> findings; validate-findings.cjs PASS; validate-coverage-ledger.cjs PASS` with the findings path `sweeps/<dir>/findings.json` a regular file under the store), `audit` (the word read from the verdict file's last line, `Verdict: correct` or `Verdict: incorrect`, never from an argument; the file `verdicts/<unit>-<sha8>.md` named by the row's unit and the head's first 8, a regular file under the store, scored by `audit-carry.py`'s predicate; evidence `class=<word> verdicts/<unit>-<sha8>.md: Blocking: <n>; Filed: <n>; Verdict: incorrect` with `--class` required, or the same without the class for correct; the verifier must start with `pr-audit.sh`), `pushed` (verdict `pushed`, verifier `coordinator (<how>)`, evidence the what) and `receipt` (verdict `-`, verifier the lever name alone from `pr-update-branch.sh`, `pr-close.sh`, `records-sync.sh`, evidence the receipt then `; <note>`). Two refusals cross rows before any write: a `pushed` or `receipt` row whose head is 40 hex while `ledger.already_audited` names an audit row of the same pull request at that head is refused naming that row's ts (the `merged` kind is exempt: the merge is the last row of a head); and any cell holding a tab or a newline is refused naming the cell. The write (U222d; rounds 2 and 3 each wrote a helper file in the store, a temporary and a lock file, which the store's own walks refuse): the lever opens `ledger.tsv` itself `O_RDWR | O_APPEND` and takes `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)` on that one descriptor (tried every 0.1 s for 30 seconds, `--lock-wait <seconds>` shortens it; expiry is a refusal naming the ledger; advisory, so `ledger.py` and `pr-merge.sh`, which take no lock, read during the hold), reads the whole text through it (`fstat` for the length, `pread` until that length), appends the new line to the text in memory and re-reads the whole through `ledger.parse_ledger`, so a row the reader would refuse is never written, then writes the one line with one `os.write` (O_APPEND puts it at the end), compares the count, fsyncs and closes, which releases the lock, so two levers writing at once never lose each other's row and the lever makes no file but the ledger and changes no mode. A write that fails or returns short, or a fsync that fails, truncates the file back to the recorded length and is exit 1 naming the ledger and the error (a truncate that fails is named too: `may hold a partial last line`); every failure of the open, the lock, `fstat` or `pread` is exit 2 with one stderr line, as is every refusal (a store that does not parse names the line); stdout `appended <ts> line <n>`; and every element of argv passes one rule, strict UTF-8 and no tab, newline or carriage return, before argparse runs (U222c's strict arguments, now ahead of `--lock-wait`'s conversion). The lever states no grammar of its own: `ledger.py` must stand beside it wherever it runs (the checkout's `scripts/orchestrator/` today; the installed copy under `~/.local/bin/common/` needs `ledger.py` installed beside it), and it is installed and pinned in `brief-check.py`'s form (the generator's copy and `claude.levers.sha256` pin), with `tests/unit/test_ledger_row.py` running it as a subprocess under `python3 -I -S` over a scratch store. Since U418 the generator also installs the reader itself, `scripts/orchestrator/ledger.py`, as the byte-identical copy `home/dot_local/bin/common/ledger.py` (the bare source name, so chezmoi places it at `~/.local/bin/common/ledger.py` with mode 0644: a module `ledger-row.py` imports and never runs) and pins it in `claude.levers.sha256` beside the writer's pin, so the deployed `python3 -I ~/.local/bin/common/ledger-row.py` finds its reader; measured 2026-11-10 before this, every deployed run refused with `no ledger.py beside the lever` and the day's rows were written from the chezmoi source clone's copy.

## `program-status.py`: the operator's state report from the store

Regime-cutover U497 (standing orders 56 and 57). The operator could not tell from the coordinator's prose whether the program was moving or stopped (2026-11-12 JST), and order 56 fixed the report's form: one word of state and its reason, a signal per goal element, the operator's acts, the decisions only the operator can make, what the agent runs. `python3 -I ~/.local/bin/common/program-status.py <store> [--ja] [--now <ts>] [--progress-root <dir>]` prints that report from the store's tables alone, and the coordinator's reply to a status question quotes the output instead of composing it. The first line is `state: <word>: <reason>, last record <age> min ago`: `done` when every `tests/*.tsv` table is green, `stopped` when nothing is in flight and an open decision or a pending operator act waits, or when the stall check reports every in-flight unit `stalled` or `no-file`, else `moving`; the reason is the first matching condition in words and the age is now minus the last `decisions.tsv` row's ts in whole minutes. Then `elements`, one line per tests table in name order (`<element>: <signal> <ok>/<rows> OK, <ng> NG, <pending> pending`, green when every verdict is `OK`, red on any `NG`, yellow on a `pending` with no `NG`, grey otherwise); `operator acts`, every tests row of kind `operator` and verdict `pending` as `<element> <id>: <command>`; `operator decisions`, every `gates.md` row under the header whose six cells read `id gate moment options default result` (read as a table row with any padding, so the kit's template and the live store both) and whose result cell is empty, as `<id>: <gate> (<moment>; default <default>)`; and `agent running`, every `in-flight` row of `units.tsv` as `<id> <track> pr <pr or -> progress: <column>`, the column from `stall-check.py` (U496) when that lever is a regular file beside this one (run once as `python3 -I <dir>/stall-check.py <store> [--progress-root <dir>] [--now <ts>]`, its rows `unit state age_minutes last_line` read as `<state>, <age_minutes> min, <last_line>`, its exit 2 a refusal of this report) and `unknown` without it. A section with no item prints `none`; `--ja` prints the five headings and the state word in the operator's forms (状態 / 要素 / 操作者の作業 / 操作者の判断 / agent が動かしているもの; 進んでいる / 止まっている / 完了) and translates nothing else. Every input is a store file with a fixed header, read by one reader each and refused on any other shape (order 21): a table whose first line is not its header or whose row has another number of cells, a `gates.md` whose first table row is not those six names, whose next is not the separator row or with a short row, a carriage return in any table (every file is read byte-exact, so a CRLF row is refused, never normalised), a `decisions.tsv` with no row, a missing or non-UTF-8 file, a store that is no directory, a `--now` that is not exactly `<YYYY-MM-DD>T<HH:MM:SS>Z` (checked as a shape before strptime), a tests table whose element name is outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, and a stall-check output that is not UTF-8 or a row of another width, state or age, each exit 2 with one stderr line (a header refusal names `<file>:1`; one sanitiser turns every line boundary inside a quoted path or value, and inside every printed cell, into a space, so the refusal stays one line and one report item is one line) and nothing on stdout; exit 0 prints the report; no file is written. Installed and pinned in `brief-check.py`'s form (the generator's copy and `claude.levers.sha256` pin, the self check first), allowed by the orchestrator policy under its installed path alone, and exercised by `tests/unit/test_program_status.py` as a subprocess under `python3 -I` over a scratch store, with a fake `stall-check.py` written beside a copy of the lever.

## `ticket-unit.py`: the Router's pull request enters the gates by its ticket

Regime-cutover U493 (handoff/44-u493-ticket-unit-design.md, table row f25). The three gate levers (the `regime` check, pr-audit.sh, pr-merge.sh) resolve a pull request's unit from the one body line `Program-Unit: <program>/U<n>`, and a pull request the Router's account opens for a ticket carries none (PR 777, ticket bf72b83b, failed the required check with no unit to name). The ticket is the unit: `~/.local/bin/common/ticket-unit.py <store> --db ~/.dsh/router-state/orchestrator.sqlite --pr <n> --branch <name> --head <sha> [--lock-wait <seconds>]`, run by the lever's own path and no interpreter prefix (its `#!/usr/bin/env -S python3 -I` line is what runs it under `python3 -I`): the orchestrator policy allows `~/.local/bin/common/ticket-unit.py *` and its next rule denies every other spelling, so a `python3 -I ~/.local/bin/common/ticket-unit.py …` command is refused by the seat under the policy's first-match rule (PR 783 Codex P1, README:130). The lever reads the ticket whose `follows` names `github:<pr>` with `own` true from the Router's sqlite (the `ticket (id, value)` table, one JSON document per row, read over `file:<db>?mode=ro` with the `sqlite3` and `json` modules; none or two such tickets is a refusal naming the count), requires `--branch` to equal `agent/` plus the ticket's `execution.name` (the second side of the binding, also the Router's own fact), holds every field it uses to a grammar before use (`id` and `decisionId` 32 hex, the sender and the actor `[A-Za-z0-9-]+`, the thread `[a-z]+:[0-9]+`, the execution name `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`, `createdAt` a timestamp; `--pr` 1 to 9 digits, `--head` 40 hex, `--branch` over `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`), and then writes two things into the store under one exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `units.tsv` in ledger-row.py's form (one `O_RDWR | O_APPEND | O_NOFOLLOW` descriptor carries the lock, the `pread` of the whole file, the check by the register's one grammar of header and eight cells, the one `write`, the `fsync`; a failed or short write truncates back and is exit 1 naming the file): the brief `briefs/U<n>-ticket-<id>.md`, created `O_CREAT | O_EXCL` at mode 0600, and the row `U<n> ticket in-flight <branch> <pr> <head> briefs/U<n>-ticket-<id>.md ticket=<id>; thread=<thread>; sender=<sender>; decision=<decisionId>; bound by ticket-unit.py <ts>`, with `U<n>` the maximum plain unit id plus one. The brief is the ticket's text as data: the sections brief-check.py requires, the ticket's `title` and `mandate.scope` inside a fenced block whose fence is a run of `~` longer than any run in the text, every line of the text prefixed with two spaces after every `str.splitlines` boundary (CR, VT, FF, FS, GS, RS, NEL, U+2028, U+2029) is turned into a newline, so no line of the text is a heading, a command or a path to any lever; SCOPE names no repository path, CLOSURE is `remove`, TRUST names the Router's admission of the sender and the gate levers' checks as what the unit trusts; the rendered brief passes `brief-check.py` against the store. Idempotent: a row whose brief cell already names this ticket's brief makes the lever print `state=existing` and write nothing (that row's brief must be a regular file under the store, and a brief file of that name with no row is a refusal: the store is inconsistent). Stdout on success is one line, `unit=U<n> brief=briefs/U<n>-ticket-<id>.md state=new|existing program=<store basename> line=Program-Unit: <program>/U<n>`, and the coordinator writes that line into the pull request's body with pr-body-edit.sh from a scratch checkout of the Router's branch, after which the `regime` check, the audit, the sweep and pr-merge.sh treat the pull request as any unit's. Every refusal is exit 2 with one stderr line (the self pin, an argument outside its grammar, the store not a directory of this user, the sqlite not a regular file or not opening read-only, no or two tickets, a field outside its grammar, the branch not the ticket's, the register malformed or reached through a link, a lock not taken in `--lock-wait` seconds, the store inconsistent), every line boundary folded into a space by program-status.py's `one_line`; the lever runs no `gh` and no git and changes no gate lever. Installed and pinned in `brief-check.py`'s form, allowed by the orchestrator policy for the installed path alone; `tests/unit/test_ticket_unit.py` runs it as a subprocess under `python3 -I` over a scratch store and a sqlite the test builds, with the real brief-check.py over the rendered brief.

## hcom-seat: start an omp seat for a role in a herdr pane

Plan 025 W5b (U7), the minimal form before X. `hcom-seat <role> [--name <name>] [--seats-root <dir>] [--ext <dir>]... [--dry-run]` starts one omp process for `orchestrator` or `worker` through `hcom omp`, which opens the herdr pane and gives the seat `HCOM_LAUNCHED=1`, `HCOM_TAG` and `HCOM_DIR` (`hcom omp --help`). There is no audit seat: `hcom-seat audit` is refused as an unknown role, and every audit is one `pr-audit.sh` run (U57 and U58 retired the seat; U119 round 3 named the Codex route). The launch line is

```text
hcom omp --tag <name> --dir <dir> --terminal herdr --go -- --profile <role> --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/<role>.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/<role>.yml --append-system-prompt ~/.agents/omp/rules/<role>.md -e <ext>...
```

`--append-system-prompt` carries the seat's standing rules, rendered per role from `standing_rules` in the manifest (U75). The overlays disable the native provider (U66), so no profile context file loads; the explicit flag is the one route no disabled provider drops, and it wins over any project `APPEND_SYSTEM.md`. omp reads a missing path as literal text, so `hcom-seat` and `omp-run.sh` (orchestrator and worker roles) refuse an absent or empty rules file.

The profile equals the role, and two overlays follow it (`--config` is repeatable). The policy overlay owns the approval mode (U6, `home/dot_agents/omp/policies/README.md`). The profile overlay carries the manifest's `modelRoles.default` (U4) and `skills.customDirectories` (U10), because a named profile reads its own `~/.omp/profiles/<role>/agent/config.yml` (pi-utils `src/dirs.ts:312-315`, measured in U10) and the default profile's rendered config never reaches a seat; U14 renders it per role. Both files are required, so no `--model` is passed. `<name>` defaults to the role and is the hcom tag; hcom derives the participant name from it (`hcom omp --help`: "names become tag-\*"), and `--name <role>-<n>` seats a second seat of a role. The `--` ends hcom's flags: hcom 0.7.30 and 0.7.31 drop it and forward every later argument to omp verbatim (`extract_launch_flags`, `src/commands/launch.rs:572-581` at tag `v0.7.30`), so the omp flags never collide with the flags hcom claims by name, including `--name`, `--system` and `--go`, which `hcom omp --help` does not list. `--go` is hcom's launch gate and the launcher always passes it, before the `--`: inside an AI tool, hcom 0.7.30 without it prints `== LAUNCH PREVIEW ==`, `Add --go to proceed.` and `[Preview Mode] Add --go to proceed with launch.` and starts nothing (`launch.rs:48`, `388-389` and `423`; the first run on the real host did exactly that for all three roles on 2026-10-23, `hcom list -v`: `No active agents`). The router sets the flag (`src/router.rs:255-256`) and `extract_launch_flags` consumes it before the `--` (`launch.rs:650-652`); after the `--` it would reach omp as a positional.

| Role           | `--dir`                                               | Default `-e` list, in order                                                                                                     |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `orchestrator` | the repository checkout the command runs in           | `~/.local/lib/omp-extensions/pi-claude-bridge`, then the SIWC when present                                                      |
| `worker`       | `<seats-root>/<name>`, a worktree of that repository  | the bridge, then the SIWC when present                                                                                          |
| `dsh`          | `<seats-root>/.state/<name>/cwd`, a scratch directory | none: no omp runs; the shim execs `<DSH_HOME>/bin/dsh` under srt                                                                |
| `router`       | `<seats-root>/.state/<name>/cwd`, a scratch directory | none: the dsh row with the DSH profile `router`, `allowLocalBinding: true` and `<DSH_HOME>/router-state` as a write root (U321) |

The first directory of a role's list is the one its model route needs (the U2 bridge for the Claude routes) and is required; the second is added only when it exists. `--ext <dir>` (repeatable) replaces the list, as `omp-run.sh` does; every directory, a default or `--ext`, must resolve to an entry of `claude.levers.extensions` in the manifest (U31, "Pre-authorized levers"), else the launch is refused. `--seats-root` defaults to `~/.local/state/omp-seats/<slug>`, the repository's own seats root (U33). A seat worktree is created with `git worktree add --detach <dir> origin/main` only when absent and reused when its git common directory is this repository's, so a restart of a seat keeps its branch and uncommitted work; any other directory at that path is refused, because the default seat path is role-named and a stale or foreign directory would seat omp in the wrong tree. The launcher never moves a seat worktree after creating it at `origin/main`. The worker moves its own tree per its brief (`git fetch origin`, then `git switch -c <branch> origin/main`, `hcom-worker` SKILL).

Every input is checked before anything is created or started, and a refusal is exit 2 with the reason on stderr: an unknown role, a name outside `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`, a store `regime-env` refuses (outside a checkout without `HCOM_DIR`, a slug whose store belongs to another checkout), hcom absent from `PATH`, an absent policy or profile overlay or extension directory, or a cwd outside a git checkout. `--dry-run` prints the launch line (shell-quoted, one line) and creates and starts nothing. Otherwise hcom's own exit status is returned (0 ready, 1 launch failed, 2 still launching or blocked on user attention).

The dsh role (regime-cutover U272). `hcom-seat dsh [--name <name>] [--seats-root <dir>] [--profile <name>] [--home <DSH_HOME>] [--dry-run] [-- <dsh args>]` starts the DeepSeek harness control host under the same containment as a worker seat, as a role whose differences from the worker are rows of the lever's role table and nothing else; the standalone launcher of PR 687 (U251 to U251d) was withdrawn because it re-implemented the environment composition this lever already carries through its audits. The rows: no pane and no hcom bus (the lever runs the shim `<sandbox>/bin/dsh` in the foreground under `/usr/bin/env -i`, waits and returns dsh's status); a per-launch seat name `<name>-<utc stamp>-<pid>`, so the sandbox, its settings file, the state slot and the bridge child's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` are per launch and two launches at once share no file; the cwd `<seats-root>/.state/<name>/cwd`, a scratch directory the lever makes (no worktree, no `.omp`); the write roots `DSH_HOME` (`--home`, else the environment's `DSH_HOME`, else `~/.dsh`), that slot, the role's own omp profile `~/.omp/profiles/dsh` and `~/.omp/run`, with no git common directory and no hcom store; `DSH_HOME` in the protected set before any tool is resolved, so a `node` or `python3` planted under it is refused like a tool under a seats root; the command `<DSH_HOME>/bin/dsh`, a physical executable file that runs inside srt only (nothing probes it outside, the U251 round-1 finding); and the environment reduced to `HOME`, `USER`, `LANG`, `TERM`, `PATH`, `DSH_HOME`, `OMP_PROFILE=dsh` and a caller's `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` before the shim adds its own exports (the token from the operator's token file, the bridge child's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the AWS file names, the signing rows). The login child `omp auth-gateway stdio` (the kit's omp-gateway plugin) writes its `agent.db` at start (measured 2026-11-06: a read-only grant on `~/.omp/agent` ends it with `SQLITE_READONLY`), so `OMP_PROFILE=dsh` puts it in the role's own profile, the worker's own-profile row, and the operator's `~/.omp/agent` stays read-denied; that profile loads no extension on its own, so the gateway's `claude-bridge` route in it is the profile-rows unit's to render (the coordinator's `seat` item in the login-route table). No omp overlay, rules file, extension or login preflight is read for the role. The per-launch sandbox and state slot are removed when dsh exits. `tests/unit/test_hcom_seat_dsh.py` holds the role's settings to the worker's with the named rows substituted, the per-launch slots, the environment names, the command refusals, the canary refusal and the exec argv.

The dsh role's profile config row (regime-cutover U310, rounds U310b to U310d). The gateway child `omp auth-gateway stdio` in the role's own profile loads its providers from that profile's `~/.omp/profiles/dsh/agent/config.yml` alone (omp 18.12.6 `src/cli/auth-gateway-stdio.ts:60-64`), so the launch places the kit's rendered overlay `~/.agents/omp/profiles/dsh.yml` there, each `extensions` entry rewritten to its canonical `claude.levers.extensions` path (`extensions_canonical`, the omp roles' `-e` rule). The lever reads the overlay and the target before anything is made (`profile_config_check`: an absent or extension-less overlay, an unsearchable directory on the path, a linked or irregular `agent/` or `config.yml` refuse with no `mkdir` at all, in a dry run too); the write is one run of `profile-config-place.py` beside the lever (`python3 -I -S`; the `python3` resolved and pinned like every tool and required for this role; the helper's sha256 read against its `claude.levers.sha256` pin first), after the per-launch slots and `seat_sandbox_write`. Three audits found one class in the lever's own bash write, symlink-write (a link at the predictable temporary name, the temporary swapped after its open, `config.yml` swapped to a directory link before `mv`), each a check over a path in `agent/`, a directory the role's own sandboxed process writes too, followed by an act on it; the helper drops the premise: it opens `agent/` once with `O_NOFOLLOW` and runs every step on that held descriptor (`dir_fd`): `fstat` (a directory of this uid with no group or other write), the read of `config.yml` (`O_NOFOLLOW`, a regular file of this uid; equal bytes are `kept` and nothing is written), the exclusive create of `.config.yml.<16 hex>`, a random name (`O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`: an entry of any kind at the name is EEXIST and a FIFO never blocks), the write and `fsync` through the new descriptor, `rename` onto `config.yml` (a link there is replaced as an entry, a directory is EISDIR) and a read-back that must hold the rendering, so a writer swapping entries around the rename ends the launch in a refusal and never in a config of other bytes. The helper's exit 2 line (the step, the path, the errno) is the lever's refusal, any other status refuses naming it, and the per-launch slots go with every refusal. A dry run runs the helper with `--dry-run` after `settings_judge` and writes nothing. Tests: `tests/unit/test_profile_config_place.py` (every step, every planted entry, every injected failure) and the U310 cases of `tests/unit/test_hcom_seat_dsh.py`.
