The deny of the same root in the acp-broker worker settings follows the root to this path on PR 720's branch; it is not on main.

### OS containment of the seat (U85)

The seat's omp runs under Anthropic's sandbox-runtime, `srt` (`npm:@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime` under mise; sandbox-exec on macOS, bubblewrap with `socat` and `ripgrep` from `install/ubuntu/common/dependencies.sh` on Linux). hcom 0.7.31 has no tool-path or wrapper setting; it resolves `omp` from the launching process's `PATH` and pins the result as the runner's `--hcom-tool-path`. So `hcom-seat` writes, on every launch, `<seats-root>/.sandbox/<name>/srt-settings.json` and the shim `<seats-root>/.sandbox/<name>/bin/omp`, and puts that `bin` first on `PATH` before `exec hcom`. The shim first runs a canary under the settings: a write into the sandbox directory and reads of the settings file and `~/.ssh`, under `/bin/sh` (dash on Ubuntu). It starts nothing (exit 2) unless all three fail inside. Then it runs `srt --settings <file> -- omp --no-extensions <omp args>`. It never falls back to an uncontained omp.

Both srt and omp run by their physical argv, with no lookup left. Every symlink is followed to the file, and a `#!` script is run by its interpreter's physical file (`#!/abs/interp` or `#!/usr/bin/env <name>`, the name resolved on `PATH` and through `mise which` for a mise shim). hcom and herdr are resolved the same way. `hcom-seat` execs hcom by that argv, and writes `<sandbox>/bin/hcom` and `<sandbox>/bin/herdr`, wrappers that exec the same argv, so hcom's own lookups of `hcom` (for the runner script) and `herdr` (the terminal preset) find them first. None of these files, nor any directory above one, may have the (device, inode) identity of a write root of any seat. The protected set comes from the same function that writes each seat's `allowWrite` list, files such as `~/.claude.json` included. It covers this seat's roots and the other role's; the seats root, every hcom store and `~/.local/state/regime-store` as a whole (U219b); every `allowWrite` entry of every existing settings file under the seats roots; and srt's implicit write paths (`/tmp/claude`, `~/.npm/_logs`, `~/.claude/debug`), so the check and the sandbox cannot drift. Each violation is refused before anything is created: a seat that could rewrite one of these files would run uncontained on the next launch.

No caller `PATH` entry runs anything (the pattern of `ci-rerun.sh`, U61). `hcom-seat`, the shim and the wrappers start with `#!/bin/bash` (bash 3.2 on macOS), so not even the interpreter comes from the caller's `PATH`, and `seat-login-preflight` is run as `/bin/bash <file>`. The first statement of `hcom-seat` sets `PATH` to `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, so the self-check's `sed` and hash tool, `awk`, `uname` and the other system tools come from there. No JSON tool writes the settings: hcom-seat builds them in the shell from clean paths. It reads existing seat settings back with `/usr/bin/plutil -extract … raw -expect array|string` on macOS (macOS 12 and later, plutil(1); macOS 14 has no system jq) and with `/usr/bin/jq` on Linux, which refuses a list with an entry that is no string or holds a newline and checks the count (from `install/ubuntu/common/dependencies.sh`; its absence there is a refusal). The tools beyond them are resolved next: `git`, `hcom`, `herdr`, `omp` and `srt` are required; `node`, `bun`, `claude`, `python3`, `regime-open-url`, `jq` and `security` are optional (`claude`, `python3` and `regime-open-url` serve `seat-login-preflight`'s interactive SSH login handoff, which calls `regime-open-url` by name; the dsh role requires `python3`, which runs its profile config helper `profile-config-place.py`; `jq` reads the openai-siwc registration there, and macOS 14 has no system jq; `security` is the preflight's only Keychain reader, so its login check reads the Keychain through that one resolved tool; hcom-seat itself runs neither); and on Linux `bwrap`, `socat` and `rg` are required too. Each is what `mise which` answers under `env -i` from `/`, else what `command -v` finds on the caller's `PATH`. Before mise or any other tool outside the system directories runs, the complete protected set is built by identity: the (device, inode) of every write root that exists, files and directories, read with `/usr/bin/stat` and `/usr/bin/jq` only. It holds the seats roots and hcom stores of every program, the program stores' root parent `~/.local/state/regime-store` whole (regime-cutover U219b: the orchestrator's write root, protected before the slug is known, so a `git` a seat plants under it is refused by name before anything runs it, in a dry run too: `test_an_executable_planted_under_the_store_root_never_runs`), `~/Workspace`, the checkout the command runs in with its git directories, the omp profiles and leases, the Claude Code state (`~/.claude.json` included), srt's implicit write paths, and every `allowWrite` entry of every existing seat settings file (another program's checkout outside `~/Workspace` included). mise's own physical file is checked against it before mise runs. Each executable is checked together with its interpreter chain, and every symlink hop of the path the kernel will follow (the executable as found, the interpreter as its `#!` line spells it) is held to the same identity check, so no hop a seat could retarget lies in a write root (`#!/abs/interp`, or `#!/usr/bin/env X` with X looked up on the `PATH` it will run with; an interpreter that is itself a script is refused), mise before it runs and each resolved tool before anything runs it. An answer runs nothing until it passes: absolute, no control character, an executable regular file once every link is followed, no shim, and neither its physical file nor any directory above it, up to `/`, has the identity of a protected root. Comparing identities, not path text, refuses a case alias on a case-insensitive file system (`~/.CLAUDE.JSON`) and a hardlink of a writable file. A hardlink the other way, from a seat's write root to a file outside it, cannot be made: srt refuses it (`ln: … Operation not permitted`, measured). A passing tool is linked into `~/.local/state/hcom-seat/tools` (0700, this user's, outside every seat write root). `PATH` then becomes that directory followed by the system directories; a trailing `:` or any other caller entry is never inherited. hcom, the shim and srt run with that `PATH` (the shim sets it again), and srt's Linux settings name `bwrapPath`, `socatPath` and `ripgrep.command` by their physical files. Inside the sandbox, `/usr/bin/env` gives omp that `PATH` followed by the caller's absolute entries, so the seat keeps its working tools (gh, uv, linters); every process there is contained. `--dry-run` also refreshes the tool links.

No seat launches a seat. Worker launches are an operator or coordinator step, run from a terminal outside every seat as gate G7 was (`hcom-orchestration` SKILL step 0). `hcom-seat` refuses (exit 2) when hcom's seat markers `HCOM_LAUNCHED` or `HCOM_PROCESS_ID` are set, or the shim's `HCOM_SEAT_SANDBOX`. It also refuses when `<seats-root>/.sandbox` exists but cannot be listed, since every seat's sandbox denies reading it; that catches a seat that unset the markers. The hard control is the sandbox itself: no seat gets the herdr socket or a write to the seats root.

|              | worker                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | orchestrator                                                                      |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| writes       | its worktree, the repository's git common directory (not `config` or `hooks`), `HCOM_DIR` (not `config.toml`, `env`, `config.env`, `.tmp/launch`, `scripts`, `integrations` or `shell_env.json`), `~/.omp/profiles/worker`, `~/.omp/run`, `<seats-root>/.state/worker` (`UV_CACHE_DIR`, and `claude`, the bridge child's Claude Code state under its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, U190); the operator's `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and a caller's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` are denied explicitly (U190b: srt 0.0.81 grants `~/.claude/debug` of the real HOME on its own, so being no root is not enough) | the same with the checkout for the worktree; never the seats root or a `.sandbox` |
| reads denied | `~/.ssh`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.aws`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.docker`, `~/.kube`, `~/.config/gcloud`, `~/.codex`, `~/.omp/agent`, other omp profiles, `~/.hcom`, other stores under `~/.local/state/hcom`, the chezmoi sources and configs, `~/Workspace` except its own repository, `<seats-root>/.sandbox`, the seats root except its own worktree, the operator's Claude Code state (`~/.claude` with its credentials file, `~/.claude.json`, a caller's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; U190b)                                                                                                                           | the same, except that it reads the worker worktrees (not `.sandbox`)              |
| network      | github.com, its API, upload, codeload and content hosts; pypi; api.anthropic.com, claude.ai, platform.claude.com, console.anthropic.com; chatgpt.com, auth.openai.com, api.openai.com; no listening socket (`allowLocalBinding: false`, see below; the router role alone grants binding, U321, "The router role"); no unix socket (the router role alone opens the broker's socket directory, U344)                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the same: no unix socket, the herdr socket included                               |

`~/.ssh` is unreadable inside, so the shim sets `remote.origin.pushurl` to the HTTPS form of the checkout's origin (through `GIT_CONFIG_*`). An explicit pushurl wins over the global `url.git@github.com:.pushInsteadOf`, so `git push origin <branch>` goes over HTTPS with the gh credential helper. The shim also sets `commit.gpgsign` and `tag.gpgsign` to false through `GIT_CONFIG_*`: seat commits are unsigned, and a host config that signs with a key under `~/.ssh` would otherwise fail every commit inside. It also sets `AWS_SHARED_CREDENTIALS_FILE` and `AWS_CONFIG_FILE` to `/dev/null`, because omp's Bedrock discovery stats `~/.aws` and dies on the read deny's `EPERM`. No seat may listen: srt 0.0.81 renders `allowLocalBinding` as `network-bind` and `network-inbound` on `local ip "*:*"` (every interface, `macos-sandbox-utils.js` around line 951), and srt has no loopback-only form. The one listener a seat runs is the hcom plugin's notify server (`hcom.ts` `startNotifyServer`, `listen(0, "127.0.0.1")`). When its bind fails the plugin logs `notify_server.start_failed` and polls for mail every 5 s instead of every 60 s (`FALLBACK_PENDING_POLL_MS`), so a delivery waits up to 5 s.

Git environment. `hcom-seat` removes every inherited `GIT_*` variable (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_CONFIG_*` and the rest) before its first git command, so host git and the seat find the repository from the working directory alone; steering git through the environment is not an input it accepts. The main checkout it protects is found by identity: the common directory's parent when that parent's `.git` is the common directory, else the main worktree `git worktree list` records (a bare repository has none, and the launch checkout stands in; for a separate git directory the launch must run in its checkout).

Host-read git state. Host git, run later by the operator or the coordinator in the checkout or a seat worktree, reads repository files that would make it run a program (`core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, filter and `textconv` drivers, includes). Every seat's `denyWrite` therefore lists the common `config`, `config.worktree`, `info/` and `hooks/`; every linked worktree's `config.worktree`, `info/`, `commondir` and `gitdir`; and the seat worktree's own `.git` file. On macOS, patterns also cover a worktree added after the launch. Before each launch, `hcom-seat` refuses state a seat may have written before these denies existed: a symbolic link among those paths, a `commondir` that does not lead to the common directory, a `.git` file that leads outside `worktrees/`, or any `config.worktree` key other than `core.sparseCheckout` and `core.sparseCheckoutCone`, read with `git config --no-includes`. This check runs before `hcom-seat`'s first git command, again before any git command in an existing seat directory, and once more when the settings are written. Every git command `hcom-seat` and the sourced `regime-env` run goes through a wrapper that runs git by its checked physical argv (`<interpreter> <script>` when git is a script): `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c extensions.worktreeConfig=false -c protocol.allow=never`. So `git worktree add` runs no `post-checkout` hook, and no `config.worktree` is read by hcom-seat's own git. A `--dry-run` validates this state and creates nothing; a launch creates an absent `config.worktree` (empty) and an absent `info/` or `hooks/` so that each deny names an existing path.

Seats roots. Every seat's `denyRead` holds the whole `~/.local/state/omp-seats` tree and every configured seats root. Its `allowRead` re-opens only its own paths: the worktree, `<seats-root>/.state/<name>`, and, for the orchestrator, its own program's seats root, where the worker clones it reviews live. srt keeps a deeper deny under an allow (measured on 0.0.81), so every `.sandbox` stays unreadable. The login directories under the seats root are denied the same way (regime-cutover U498c): every seat's `denyRead` names, by name, each other login group's `<seats-root>/.state/claude-<group>` (the range of `login_group_of_role`, `LOGIN_GROUPS`) and the legacy `.state/claude`, so the orchestrator's read grant of the seats root re-opens no other group's login, and the own group's directory is the one login a seat reads or writes.

Worker reads of the coordinator's records. A worker's `denyRead` holds the main checkout itself, wherever it lives (derived from the git common directory, not from `~/Workspace`). Its `allowRead` re-opens only the checkout's git directory (the worktree's objects and refs), `<checkout>/.orchestration/orchestrate/**` (the records until U221 moves them) and the program store's root `REGIME_STORE_ROOT` (`<physical HOME>/.local/state/regime-store/<slug>`, regime-env's derivation, U215c; regime-cutover U219, store move 3 of 5: the root in its physical form, held once by `store_root_hold`), so it reads briefs and verdict files by absolute path wherever the records live. The orchestrator reads the same root and is the one role that writes it: its `allowWrite` row 7, the held slot after `~/.omp/run`, printed verbatim from the one hold and never resolved again (U219c, verdict U219-985b7c04 P1 `:2161`: round 2 resolved it a second time where the roots are rendered, so a directory-to-link swap of the root between the two reads rendered the link's target as the orchestrator's write root). Every other row, the worker's, the dsh's and the router's, carries the root in `denyWrite` (the credential root's shape, U353d: the held physical path, tagged `store`), and the dsh and router rows carry no grant of it. One judgement, `store_root_judge`, the first step of `settings_judge` for the dsh and router rows (launch and dry run) and the omp roles' judgement right after their render, holds every rendered `allowWrite` row of every role against the root by identity (`identity_table`, `within_ix`: a row that is, contains or lies inside the root is refused by name before the settings file is written), the orchestrator's own held row excepted by its position and byte equality alone (`row_tag`, so a profile directory or a `~/.omp/run` linked to the root, or a seats root inside it, launches nothing; U219c, the same verdict's P1 `:1981`: round 2 judged `HCOM_DIR` alone). The root is made by every role's launch that finds it absent, `regime-store` and `<slug>` each by its own `mkdir` under `umask 077`, after the judgement and before the settings file is written (`store_root_make`, the shape of `cred_root_make`), and each component is held, present or made, to a real directory of this user whose mode is 0700 (`/usr/bin/stat`, its status read; a link, a file or another mode is refused by name at the hold, launch and dry run alike, as regime-init refuses it; a dry run makes nothing and says what a launch would make), because srt 0.0.81's Linux backend skips an absent write path and binds nothing for an absent deny (U219c, P2 `:1815`: the first `regime-init` inside a Linux orchestrator seat could not have written a root nothing had made, and the worker's deny of it would have been void). An hcom store `HCOM_DIR` that is, lies inside or contains the root is refused by name before anything is made, in a dry run too (`store_root_overlap_check`, by identity for what exists and by the absent components below one shared ancestor otherwise: the hcom store is every seat's write grant, so the records would lie inside a worker's write root; verdict U215-15c6be1e P1). The rest of the checkout and of `~/Workspace` stays unreadable.

Clean paths. srt 0.0.81 reads every settings path as a pattern (a Seatbelt regex on macOS), so `HCOM_DIR=/private/tmp/hcom*` would grant writes to its siblings. Every input path (`HOME`, `--seats-root`, `HCOM_DIR`, `REGIME_SEATS_ROOT`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) and every path written into the settings must be clean: absolute, made of components in `[A-Za-z0-9._@+-]`, none of them empty, `.` or `..`, and no trailing `/`. Anything else is refused before anything is created. The one exception is the macOS worktree deny patterns, a clean prefix with a fixed `worktrees/*/<file>` suffix.

Non-overlapping roots. One invariant is checked over each seat's final `allowWrite` list (the launching seat's and the orchestrator's) before anything is created, dry runs included. For every granted root R and every protected path P, R must not be or contain P, and R must not lie inside P unless it is the seat's own slot there. The protected paths are the main checkout, the seats root, its `.sandbox`, every other seat directory under the seats root and every other worktree of the repository; the own slots are `<seats-root>/<name>`, `<seats-root>/.state/<name>` and a plain checkout's own `.git`. Both sides are physical and must be clean. A linked worktree inside a seat's write root, such as Claude Code's `<checkout>/.claude/worktrees/<name>` inside the orchestrator's checkout, refuses the launch, present or absent, dry runs included, naming the worktree and `git worktree move`, `remove` or `prune` (operator decision U95-nested-refuse-decided). Before any git command, the launch checkout's `.git` must be a regular file or a real directory, the git directory it names a real directory, and its `commondir` absent or a regular file (required in a linked worktree's), so a FIFO or link there cannot steer or block git. Every path read from a git pointer file (a `.git` file's `gitdir:`, a `commondir`, a `gitdir` back-pointer) must be in git's own form before any type test: no trailing `/`, no `.` component, no empty component, and `..` only leading a relative path. Every worktree's git directory `<common>/worktrees/<id>` is enumerated once, dot names included as git's own readdir does, held one to one to the worktrees git lists, and checked in git's shape: a real directory holding `commondir` (without it git takes that directory for its own common directory, `setup.c` `get_common_dir_noenv`) and none of the names git keeps in the common directory (`config`, `hooks`, `objects`, `packed-refs` and the rest of `path.c`'s common list); each git file hcom-seat reads is checked to be a regular file before its size or content, so a FIFO is refused instead of read as empty. A worktree record that is not directory-backed (its path, or its deepest existing ancestor, a regular file or a dangling link) refuses, naming `git worktree prune` and `git worktree repair`. A dry run on a checkout with 300 registered worktrees finishes in under 10 s on this host (measured: 8.8 to 9.1 s, 2.5 s with none), against 112 s on f1374ba7. The overlap and git-state checks resolve worktree records in batches. The count tests pin the measured shapes (present, absent, seats) as regressions, not as a general guarantee. This refuses `--seats-root <checkout>/seats`, an hcom store inside the seats root, and a separate git directory (`--separate-git-dir`) that holds the seats root. The checkout and its git directories are checked clean before the first git command, so a checkout path srt could misread creates no metadata.

Boundary. The sandbox contains the seat process. Host processes (the operator's shell, the coordinator, host git, editors) run as the same user, and they still read files the seat may write: its worktree's tracked files and every path in its `allowWrite`. srt is the boundary for the seat process only. Host-side consumers of seat-writable paths are the accepted G17 residual: the operator decided on 2026-10-26, verbatim, "G17 は U85 マージ後、srt を境界として受け入れる。条件は上記3点", with three conditions: (1) seats run on this repository only, and untrusted input moves to a VM; (2) seat output enters only through the PR diff, CI and the U82 apply container; (3) widening the allowed network domains reopens G17. The class covers interpreter and link chains through seat write roots (directory aliases included), a tracked script the operator later runs, a `.envrc` or editor config in a worktree, and a nested repository inside a worktree. The host-side checks in `hcom-seat` stay as defence in depth and are best effort, not closed: the identity checks on tools, interpreters and link hops, the git state checks (`git_state_check`), and the trusted interpreter directories for the optional tools (an optional tool's `#!` interpreter must be spelled in `/bin`, `/usr/bin`, `/sbin`, `/usr/sbin` or `~/.local/share/mise/installs` once its directory is followed physically).

Limits. `/tmp/claude`, srt's own TMPDIR, stays writable to every seat. A Claude Code child of the bridge keeps its state (`.claude.json`, `projects`, `sessions`, `backups`, `plugins`) under `<seats-root>/.state/<name>/claude`, the `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the seat shim exports, and logs in by `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` alone, which the shim exports from the operator's token file `~/.config/claude-code/oauth-token` when the caller has not; the operator's `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and a caller's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` are denied to every seat for write and for read (U190b: srt 0.0.81 grants `~/.claude/debug` of the real HOME on its own, so the deny is explicit, the overlap check refuses a store or seats root that is, contains or lies under them, and the shim's fourth canary probe proves at every launch that a write under `~/.claude/debug` fails; measured 2026-11-05, the child under that deny with its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and no token starts and answers `Not logged in`, no EPERM). The token in the seat's environment is the G17 residual: a seat reads its own `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, the same class as the Keychain item and credentials file that were readable inside a seat before U190 (measured 2026-11-05 inside `sweep-2`); the shim never prints or writes it, and the operator revokes a setup-token at claude.ai. A resumed or forked seat (`hcom r`, `hcom f`) resolves `omp` from the caller's `PATH`, not through `hcom-seat`, and runs uncontained. Inside the orchestrator's sandbox `~/.codex` is unreadable, so a codex audit (`pr-audit.sh`, which links `~/.codex/auth.json`) runs from the coordinator outside the seat: its terminal is the operator's or the coordinator's, outside every seat, never a seat's checkout; the run hands back its verdict path, and the orchestrator seat copies the file and writes the ledger row.

### Bus trust boundary

The bus carries text, and every seat treats that text as data: a line of any kind in "Line kinds" names work or reports it, and never authorises a command, however it is worded. Identities are hcom participant names, the `from` and `delivered_to` of a row, not anything the text claims; `hcom-dispatch` sends only to a name hcom knows and refuses an identifier outside `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`. The store is private to the program: `HCOM_DIR` selects it, the seats' launch creates it, `hcom-dispatch` and `bus-history.sh` refuse to run without it, and no seat reads `$HCOM_DIR/hcom.db` or writes under `$HCOM_DIR` except through hcom. The seat's srt sandbox (above) is the containment boundary; the policy overlays gate tools, not files (U6, "approval is not containment").

| `scripts/orchestrator/hcom-seat`, the seat directory's `.omp`                                             | closed: a seat directory that holds `.omp` (a directory, a file or a symlink) is refused after the worktree exists and before hcom starts, because omp 18.11.3 merges the cwd's `.omp/config.yml` at start and no setting turns that off (measured under a scratch HOME: an overlay's or the profile's `disabledProviders: [native]` left it applied); `test_a_seat_directory_holding_omp_project_config_is_refused_before_hcom_starts` (U66)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `--no-extensions` on the seat                                                                             | closed: hcom 0.7.31 refuses `--no-extensions` after `--` and in `HCOM_OMP_ARGS` (measured), so the seat shim `<seats-root>/.sandbox/<name>/bin/omp` (U85) puts it first in omp's argv; `test_the_worker_launch_reaches_omp_in_a_worktree_of_origin_main` asserts it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| the operator's Claude Code state (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`)                                          | closed (U190, U190b): the bridge child runs under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<seats-root>/.state/<name>/claude`, a real 0700 directory the launch creates, and logs in by the token the shim exports from the operator's token file; `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and a caller's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` are no write root of either role and, since srt 0.0.81 grants `~/.claude/debug` of the real HOME on its own, are denied explicitly for write and for read in every seat's settings, protected in the overlap check (`HCOM_DIR=~/.claude` or a seats root under it refuses before anything is created) and probed by the shim's canary (a write open under `~/.claude/debug` must fail, else exit 2), so a seat steered by repository or bus content can add no user-scope MCP server or rule the operator's next unsandboxed session would start, and reads no credentials file; `test_omp_runs_under_srt_behind_a_canary_with_the_seat_roots` (both deny lists, both roles), `test_a_root_in_the_operators_claude_state_is_refused`, `test_a_canary_that_finds_the_sandbox_open_starts_no_omp`, `test_the_shim_exports_the_token_file_into_the_seat_and_never_prints_it`, `test_the_shim_refuses_when_the_token_file_cannot_be_read`, `test_the_shim_keeps_a_token_the_caller_already_exported`, `test_a_remote_seat_without_a_login_starts_nothing` (a Keychain login alone starts no seat, on a local host too); residual: the token is readable inside the seat, the same class as the Keychain item and credentials file were (measured 2026-11-05), and the operator revokes a setup-token at claude.ai; owner: U190                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| the `dsh` role (U272)                                                                                     | closed: `tests/unit/test_hcom_seat_dsh.py`: `test_the_dsh_settings_are_the_workers_but_the_named_rows` (every list the worker's but the substituted rows), `test_two_launches_at_once_share_no_settings_file_and_each_slot_goes_with_its_dsh` (the U251d concurrency finding), `test_the_environment_reaching_dsh_is_the_listed_names_and_the_shims_exports` (`SECRET_OF_THE_CALLER` and `NODE_OPTIONS` reach neither the canary nor the launch nor dsh), `test_the_command_and_its_home_are_resolved_and_refused_like_a_tool` (a planted `node` and `python3` under `DSH_HOME` refused, never run), `test_a_canary_that_finds_the_sandbox_open_starts_no_dsh`, `test_srt_receives_the_canary_and_the_exec_argv_and_a_dry_run_creates_nothing`; residual: the `srt --version` probe runs under the operator's own terminal environment for every role (the G17 trusted side)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| the `router` role (U321)                                                                                  | closed: `tests/unit/test_hcom_seat_dsh.py`: `test_the_router_settings_are_the_dsh_settings_but_the_loopback_grant_and_the_state_root` (the structural diff is the binding value and allowWrite row 6 and nothing else; `OMP_PROFILE=dsh` and `--profile router` reach dsh), `test_the_router_role_runs_the_router_profile_alone` (another profile refused by name before any tool is resolved), `test_a_router_dry_run_prints_the_launch_line_with_the_router_profile_and_makes_no_slot`, `test_the_router_canary_refuses_like_the_dsh_roles_and_both_slots_go`, `test_the_dsh_settings_are_byte_identical_to_the_u318_head` (the dsh role's settings unchanged by the row); by design: the grant is every interface (srt 0.0.81 has no loopback-only form), bounded by the upstream Management API's host check; residual: the Router's first real launch under the role is the coordinator's stage A of U317                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

`tests/unit/test_hcom_seat.py` runs the launcher against `tests/fixtures/hcom-seat/fake-tool.py` under the names `hcom`, `herdr` and `omp`: the fake hcom records its argv, previews and starts nothing without `--go`, and plays the herdr terminal preset (`herdr tab create --label <tag>`, then `herdr pane run <pane> ...`), so the test asserts the exact hcom argv, the omp argv after `--`, omp's cwd and `HCOM_*` environment, the worktree's head, and that every refusal and the dry run create and start nothing. Every run uses a scratch `HOME` and `HCOM_DIR=$TMPDIR/u7-hcom`; the real hcom, herdr and omp are never started by the tests.

## The ACP broker: `acp-broker` and its thin client `acp-seat`

Regime-cutover U343 (M-24 part 1; the U342 design note's shape A). srt does not nest (M-22), so the Router, the dsh-superagent control host that `hcom-seat router` runs inside srt, cannot start a worker sandbox of its own, while R-45 requires the Router and every worker in separate sandboxes. `acp-broker <seats-root> <router seat> <router scratch cwd>` runs outside every sandbox as the operator's user (started by `hcom-seat` beside the Router from a private per-launch copy of its lever set, each file hashed against its pin, with the broker's `acp-broker: listening on <sock>` line as the readiness sign, U344 and U344c; refused inside a seat by the three markers) and listens on `<seats-root>/.state/<router seat>/broker/sock`, a Unix stream socket (socket 0600; the directory is `hcom-seat`'s, regime-cutover U343i: the router launch makes it 0700 before the Router's settings are written and those settings deny the Router every write under it, and the broker takes it as made, present, no link, this user's, 0700 and empty, else refuses by name and makes nothing there, so one listener per Router launch: a socket or any other entry inside refuses, the entries read by a glob so that a name of any bytes counts, one newline alone included (U343j), and since the take is a check before the bind, two brokers started together can both pass it and the bind decides: the relay that loses it refuses `cannot bind the broker socket` and exits 2, and its broker removes nothing when the socket stands after the relay's end, the winner's socket and records untouched, exit 2 naming the socket (U343j: the socket is the relay's alone to bind and to remove)). The router row's `network.allowUnixSockets` cell is U344's: `hcom-seat` renders `["<seats-root>/.state/<router seat>/broker"]` for the router row and `[]` for every other role, so the Router reads the broker directory and connects to the socket in it, which U342 measured as the only thing such a row opens (a worker under the dsh row reaches no loopback port and no Unix socket); the broker tests' synthetic Router settings write that row by hand, and the two real-launch compares read it from `hcom-seat`. Inside the Router's sandbox `dsh-subagent-acp` spawns `acp-seat --socket <path> --repo <path> --ref <rev> --name <name>`, which refuses outside a seat (`HCOM_SEAT_SANDBOX` unset), connects, sends one header line and relays its stdin and stdout to the socket until either side closes; it never runs srt. Per connection the broker reads the header `ACP-SEAT v1 repo=<path> ref=<rev> name=<name>` (regime-cutover U343e, the mechanism-change row of 2026-11-07T09:41:47Z: the Router names a repository and a ref, never a path that becomes a grant; the repository a clean absolute path, the ref a revision word, the name in the seat-name grammar and neither `claude`, the Router's own seat name nor one beginning `acp-`, nothing after the fifth field, the line and its newline within 1024 characters; anything else is one `acp-broker: refused: <reason>` line and the connection closed, nothing made), makes the per-connection slot `<seats-root>/.state/acp-<name>-<utc stamp>-<pid>/` (0700, a root the Router's sandbox denies for read and no seat may write) and clones the ref into `<slot>/task`: the repository is read inside the Router's own sandbox (`srt --settings <the Router's rendered settings> -- git …`, the pinned physical git under the isolated library's environment and flags, so a gitfile, an alternates file or a link in the Router's repository reaches only what the Router itself may read), `rev-parse --verify <ref>^{commit}` then `pack-objects --revs --stdout` of that commit onto a descriptor the broker opened in the slot, and the isolated git outside indexes the pack into a fresh repository in `task` (`--template=`, `index-pack --strict`) and checks the commit out detached; a ref that does not resolve or a pack that does not index refuses by name with the slot removed. It renders the worker's settings into `<slot>/settings.json`, runs the four-word canary of the `hcom-seat` shim under them by the physical srt argv (any other answer refuses, the slot removed, no omp), appends `<utc> acp-broker: canary <four words>` to `<seats-root>/.state/broker-logs/<name>.log`, in a directory the broker makes 0700 under its own `.state` that no seat's settings grant (regime-cutover U343o, round 12; verdict U343n-fd3db1b3 P1 `acp-broker:1264`, the record-path premise once more: until round 12 the log was `<seats-root>/.state/<name>/acp-launch.log`, inside the state slot an hcom-seat seat of that name writes, and a hard link a seat planted at that name passed the `-L` test and redirected the unsandboxed append to a protected file; the broker makes and writes nothing under `.state/<name>` any more, and a header name `broker-logs` is refused like `claude`), reads omp's version under the same settings and logs `omp acp start omp <version> cwd <task> repo <path> ref <rev> base <commit>`, then runs `srt --settings <slot>/settings.json -- omp acp --profile dsh` in `task` with the connection as its stdin and stdout, in a process group of its own so the reaper ends omp with every descendant in that group (TERM to the group, a 5 s grace polled with `kill -0`, then KILL). When omp exits the worker's HEAD goes back into the repository as `refs/acp-seat/<name>/<stamp>-<pid>` the way the clone came, across two boundaries and nothing between them: the worker's HEAD is read and the commits above the base packed inside the worker's own sandbox (`srt --settings <slot>/settings.json -- git …` on `task`, U343f: the worker may write everything under `task`, `.git` included, so an alternates file, a gitfile or a HEAD it plants reaches only what the worker may read and the push fails there instead of importing it), and `index-pack --strict --stdin` and `update-ref` (empty old value) run inside the Router's own sandbox, the pack between them on descriptors the broker opened in the slot; a HEAD equal to the base pushes nothing (`push=none`), a failed step is named on stderr (`push=failed`), a teardown by signal pushes nothing (`push=skipped`). The exit report is a record file `<seats-root>/.state/<router seat>/broker/<id>` (`<id>` the slot name; regime-cutover U343g, form `remove`), in the Router's socket directory, which the Router's settings grant and no worker setting reaches, written by the connection's reaper alone through the pinned helper `acp-record-place.py` on a held descriptor of that directory (U343h, the paragraph below; `ref=refs/acp-seat/<name>/<stamp>-<pid>` and `reaper=<pid>` as soon as the slot exists, then `status=<omp's status, or 2 when the slot remains> push=<word>` at the end): the stream carries exactly one broker line, the first, `acp-broker: record <id>` (or the refusal), every later byte is the worker's and reaches the client unread, and the client reads the record after the socket closes, prints `acp-seat: push=<word>` on stderr, unlinks it and exits with its status (exit 2 naming `<id>` when the record is missing or incomplete). The teardown is that reaper, a subshell forked the moment the slot exists, before the settings write and the clone (regime-cutover U343o; verdict U343n-fd3db1b3 P1 `acp-broker:1581`: until round 12 it was forked after the canary and the version read, and a signal to the handler during the clone ended a process with no trap and stranded the slot), outside omp's process group with HUP, INT and TERM ignored, holding the connection's stdout and reading a control pipe from the handler. The handler proceeds only on the reaper's one line `ready <pid>`, read with a 10 s bound on a second pipe in the slot and written after the record's running form was placed (P1 `acp-broker:1657`: until round 12 the record's existence was the sign, and a helper that renamed the record into place and then failed left a visible record while the reaper exited 1, so omp started with no reaper; a reaper that died, failed its placement or stayed silent refuses the connection, `the reaper (pid N) did not report ready within 10 s`), and from the fork on the reaper alone removes the slot, in every path of its own (a failed placement; EOF or `done` before any `start`; `done` or EOF after it): on the handler's `done` line or on the pipe's EOF (the handler died, by the relay's signal or otherwise, or refused after the fork) it ends omp's group when one was started, logs the exit line when omp ran, removes the slot and writes the record's final line (`status=2 push=skipped` when omp never ran) before it closes the connection; the handler's one trap (regime-cutover U343p, round 13; verdict U343o-c5c2b968 P1 `acp-broker:1648`, class handler-window, form remove: a TERM between the slot's `mkdir` and the fork still stranded the slot) is armed right before the slot's `mkdir` and disarmed right after the ready line, removes the slot with the same idempotent `slot_remove` and exits 2, and touches neither omp nor the record, so the slot has an owner at every instant, no trap on HUP, INT or TERM exists in a connection once the reaper has reported ready, and no signal to the handler reaches the removal or the report; what the reaper cannot end, a descendant that left omp's process group and itself under SIGKILL, are the PR body's residual rows. The relay, `acp-relay.py` beside the lever, is the one non-bash part: run as `python3 -I -S` by its pinned physical argv after the broker checked its `claude.levers.sha256` pin (and it checks the same pin itself), it binds the socket, starts `acp-broker --connection` per accepted connection (the start and the registration under the one lock its stop takes) and copies bytes both ways, interpreting nothing; a hangup, interrupt or termination ends every live worker through its handler's reaper; the relay removes the socket it bound at its end, and the broker removes the records no client read once the socket is gone (a socket that stands after the relay's end is another broker's listener, or one the relay could not remove: the broker removes nothing and exits 2 naming it), while the directory stands, `hcom-seat`'s, until the launch's slot removal takes it with the Router's state slot.

The worker's settings are the dsh row's shape with the worker's cells. The deny table is read from the Router's own settings, `<seats-root>/.sandbox/<router seat>/srt-settings.json`, which `hcom-seat` rendered for this launch (the router row is the dsh row with the loopback grant and the state root, U321, so its `denyRead` and `denyWrite` are the dsh row's deny table for this seats root, checkout, store and host): every row must be a clean path (the macOS worktree patterns, a clean prefix and the fixed `worktrees/*/<file>` suffix, are the one exception, as in `hcom-seat`), the rows under the Router's scratch cwd (its project-file denies) are re-rooted under the slot's task, and `denyRead` gains the Router's credential root `<DSH_HOME>/router-credentials` as its last row, where the dsh row renders it (regime-cutover U353, U343l): the broker holds the root from `DSH_HOME` in its own environment, the one `hcom-seat` sets for the Router's sandbox and for the broker it starts, in `hcom-seat`'s form (a clean physical path under the physical home, a real directory of this user, no link, else refused by name; an unset `DSH_HOME` refuses too), and the Router's own `denyWrite` must hold that root, else the connection refuses before anything is made (`hcom-seat` and the broker would disagree on the root). The grants are the broker's and the role's own: `allowWrite` the slot's task, the shared Claude Code directory `<seats-root>/.state/claude` (U318), `~/.omp/profiles/dsh`, `~/.omp/run` and srt's own writable paths; `allowRead` the slot's task, the shared directory, that profile, `~/.agents/skills` and the private tool directory; no row names the Router's scratch cwd, its state slot or the repository. The slot cannot lie under `.sandbox`: the worker carries the Router's `.sandbox` deny row, and srt 0.0.81's macOS profile emits a `denyWrite` row after the `allowWrite` roots, where the later rule wins (measured by U343e), so a write root there would be denied. No DSH_HOME and no state slot (the worker is omp, not dsh, and the launch log is the broker's to write); `allowLocalBinding` false, `allowUnixSockets` empty. The worker runs under `/usr/bin/env -i` with `HOME`, `PATH` (the private tool directory `~/.local/state/hcom-seat/tools` and the system directories outside; inside, those followed by the caller's absolute entries, the construction of `hcom-seat`'s seat path), `OMP_PROFILE=dsh`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<seats-root>/.state/claude`, the AWS file names omp's Bedrock discovery needs, `HCOM_SEAT_SANDBOX=<slot>` and, when the broker's caller exported them, `USER` and `LANG`; the Router-side git runs get `HOME` and the outer `PATH` alone. The worker's environment also carries `REGIME_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE`, the seats' API key file as `hcom-seat`'s router row set it in the broker's own environment (regime-cutover U516; handoff/47, U510, U512), whose physical path is the worker's last `allowRead` row and whose bytes the broker never reads: an unset or empty name, a relative path, a symbolic link, a path that is not a regular file of this user, a mode other than 0600 or a spelling whose physical path is not clean refuses that worker's connection before the settings writer is sourced and before anything of the worker is made, one line on the stream and on stderr whatever the path holds (a line boundary in it is folded to a space, and the mode read's and the resolver's own diagnostics are captured, so a key removed or a directory made unsearchable under the hold is that one line too, U516d) naming the variable and `handouts/anthropic-api-key-u510.md`, exit 2 of that connection; the key is a worker's precondition, not the broker's (U516c): the listener starts whatever the variable holds, so a launcher that predates U512 still runs the Router and every worker request fails closed, so the Router's subagent turns run on the key as every other bridge turn does, or not at all. srt, omp, python3 and git are taken from the private tool directory, else from the caller's PATH, and run by their physical argv (every link followed, a `#!` script by its physical interpreter, no shim, nothing under the seats root or the scratch cwd; git a binary named `git`). The settings text is the one writer's, `seat-settings-render.sh` (U344), which both levers source from a private copy hashed against its pin (U344c; the path rule every row obeys, the fixed deny rows, the domain list and the JSON text; every root is the calling lever's argument and none is baked in): the worker's file is the dsh row's file with the worker's rows in the role's positions, byte for byte, and `tests/unit/test_acp_broker.py` holds it twice, `test_the_worker_settings_are_the_dsh_row_but_the_named_rows` (the deny lists byte-identical to the Router's and, the per-launch names and the cwd normalised, to a dsh launch's on the same host; the grant lists the dsh row's minus its state slot and DSH_HOME rows) and `test_one_settings_writer_renders_the_worker_and_the_dsh_row_byte_for_byte` (the Router's and the dsh row's settings from real held `hcom-seat` launches on the fixture, the worker's from a connection to the broker that launch started; each file re-serialises to its own bytes; the whole worker file equal to the dsh row's file with the worker's rows). The fork's Executor (M-26) consumes the new header; it is not changed here.

The exit record's write (regime-cutover U343h, round 6; verdict U343g-8a049561 P1 `acp-broker:1262`, class record-path). The broker directory lies in the Router's own state slot, which the Router's settings granted for write until U343i, and the Router learns `<id>` from the stream's first line, so round 5's `printf '%s\n' "$@" > "${record}.tmp"` in the reaper, an unsandboxed open of a path under that directory, followed a link the Router had planted at `<id>.tmp` and overwrote a protected host file (the auditor reproduced it with scratch files; the record's `mv -f` had the same shape: a link at `<id>` to a directory moved the temporary into that directory). The premise, an unsandboxed process opening a path for write inside a directory a sandboxed party writes, is the one U310 retired for `hcom-seat`'s profile write, and the broker now has the same form: no `>` redirection, `mv`, `cp` or `ln` of the broker's bash names a path under the broker directory, and both forms of the record are written by `acp-record-place.py <broker-dir> <dev>:<ino> <id>` beside the lever, the record's lines on its stdin (the form of `profile-config-place.py`, U310d and U310f). The listener reads the broker directory's device and inode once, right after its own `mkdir` (`stat`, its status read), and carries the value to every connection in the relay's environment (`ACP_BROKER_DIR_ID`); the helper opens `<broker-dir>` once with `O_NOFOLLOW` (`O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`), refuses unless `fstat` gives that identity, this user and no group or other write bit, and then, relative to that held directory descriptor alone (`dir_fd` on every call), unlinks `<id>.tmp` (a planted link, file or FIFO goes as an entry, its target untouched), creates it with `O_EXCL` and `O_NOFOLLOW` (`O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`) 0600, writes the bytes (at most 4096, newline-terminated), `fsync`s, and `rename`s it onto `<id>` with both directory descriptors the held one (a link at `<id>`, to a file or to a directory, is replaced as an entry and never followed; a directory there fails), then `fsync`s the directory; `<id>` must match the slot grammar, so no `/` reaches any `os` call. A refusal is one stderr line and exit 2 with nothing created; a failure after the create is exit 1 with the temporary unlinked through the descriptor. The reaper runs it through a hold (`record_place`): the path beside the lever is no link and this user's regular file with no group or other write bit (`record_helper_path_check`, run by the handler before the slot is made), a private copy 0600 is made in a directory the reaper makes 0700 under `hcom-seat`'s own state directory `~/.local/state/hcom-seat/` (where `hcom-seat` keeps its tool links and its own helper copies; no seat's settings grant a write there), the copy's sha256 must equal the helper's `claude.levers.sha256` pin in the manifest the self-check read, and the pinned `python3 -I -S` runs that copy, its stdout sent to stderr and its status read; a hold or a helper that fails writes nothing: before the record line the handler refuses and no omp runs, after it the record keeps its running form and the client exits 2 naming `<id>`. Tests: `tests/unit/test_acp_record_place.py` (every step, every planted entry, every injected failure) and the U343h cases of `tests/unit/test_acp_broker.py` (a link at `<id>.tmp` and at `<id>` planted after the record line with the fake srt, a helper altered beside the lever).

## Pre-authorized levers

Regime-cutover U16 (brief `F8-coordinator-levers-preauthorized`). The Claude Code coordinator runs the regime's own operations from inside its sandbox, without a classifier round trip, through the `permissions.allow` rules in the managed settings (`claude.permissions.allow` in `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml`, one row each in the table below). Auto mode resolves an action that matches a rule before the classifier: "Actions matching your allow, ask, or deny rules resolve immediately" (code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes, "How the classifier evaluates actions"), and it suspends only the allow rules that "could run arbitrary code: tool-wide and wildcard rules such as `Bash(*)`, and interpreter or shell-wrapper prefixes such as `Bash(python *)`" (code.claude.com/docs/en/settings, `autoMode.classifyAllShell`, default `false` and unset here). So every rule names one deterministic wrapper that validates its own input, and nothing wider.

Since U31 each lever rule names the copy chezmoi installs under `~/.local/bin/common`, outside every sandbox write root: the generator renders `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_<lever>` from `scripts/orchestrator/<lever>` (same bytes) and pins its sha256 as `claude.levers.sha256.<lever>` in the manifest. At start each lever hashes its own bytes and compares the digest with its pin in the manifest beside it, the deployed `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml` for the installed copy; a mismatch, a missing pin or any failed read exits 2 naming the file. `hcom-seat` loads only the directories in `claude.levers.extensions`. So the rules render on Linux and WSL2 too; until U31 part 2, part 1 rendered them into the darwin variant only (`claude.permissions.darwin_allow`, D-024-144).

and the hook's execute bit), then renamed into place, the hook first; then removes a round-1 `pre-commit` or `post-checkout` that is byte-equal to round 1's (`removed`) and leaves any other file at those paths alone; a second run prints `unchanged` for each, so `regime-init` calls it on every run; `check <checkout>`: the same refusals, then `installed`, `absent`, `differs`, `older` or `not-executable` for the hook, `installed`, `absent` or `differs` for the marker, and `stale` for a round-1 hook still there, exit 0 only when both are installed and nothing is stale; every row is `checkout-guard: <state><TAB><path>`; the hook body and the marker line are heredocs of this file, so its pin covers them. The hook is a `python3 -I -S` script (no shell starts, so no shell startup file, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASH_ENV` or `PYTHON*` variable reaches it); in the `prepared` phase of every reference transaction it reads the `<old-value> SP <new-value> SP <ref-name> LF` lines (each value 40 or 64 lowercase hex or `ref:<ref-target>`, else the line is refused naming the value, U135), and while the marker lies beside it as a regular file (read by `os.lstat` from the hook's own directory, the one git ran it from: a symbolic link, whatever it points at, a directory or any other kind is no marker, an absent one is none, any other read failure refuses the transaction) or `.orchestration/orchestrate/*/units.tsv` exists under the toplevel of the worktree the command runs in or under the main worktree's (the pre-move trigger, which U221 removes; `os.listdir` of `.orchestration`, then `orchestrate`, then every program directory, in that order and to the last: a listing and never a `stat` of a child, which a mode-0111 directory answers; an absent `.orchestration` or `orchestrate` is no store, any other `OSError` on any of those listings refuses naming the directory; the paths from git as bytes, never split on newlines; the main checkout is this worktree when its git directory is the common directory, else the parent of a common directory named `.git`, as `git worktree list` derives it, and from a linked worktree whose common directory is not named `.git` every move is refused naming it, since git records no name for the main checkout there; U135 removed the `worktree list` read, which named a separate git directory reached through a `.git` symlink as the main worktree) it refuses the transaction with the route to `worker-worktree.sh` when any line moves a `refs/heads/*` ref to a commit no `refs/remotes/origin/*` ref reaches (`git --no-replace-objects -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null merge-base --is-ancestor` under `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, each status read, so no `refs/replace/*` ref and no `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE` rewrites the ancestry; a git read that exits non-zero refuses); deletions, `ref:` values, a line whose new value is the commit the ref already names (a backend update: `pack-refs` and `gc` write a packed ref as `<zero> <value> <ref>`, so the ref is read when the old value is zero) and every ref outside `refs/heads/` pass, the other phases exit 0. Left out: a worker that edits `.git/hooks` (the worker tree is a repository of its own and never sees this hook or the marker), a branch made a symbolic ref, a copy or rename of a branch already off origin, and git resolving its hooks to another place under the caller's configuration or environment, whenever and however set (U129 TRUST: the worker is a non-adversarial actor whose reflex the refusal stops; each of those is a standing-order breach the records catch, not a defect the hook closes), a commit on a detached HEAD (no branch moves), a repository without an `origin` remote (every branch move is refused there while the marker or a store exists), the operator's own removal of the marker (the one way the guard goes inert in a checkout, by hand); the isolated git of the levers (`-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`) never runs it | `tests/unit/test_checkout_guard.py`, `tests/unit/test_lever_self_check.py`    |

Run each lever by its installed path, exactly as written (`~/.local/bin/common/<lever> ...`), with the checkout root as the working directory (`hcom-seat` and `worktree-gc.sh` read the repository from it): the rules name only that spelling, so `scripts/orchestrator/<lever>`, `./scripts/...` or the expanded `/Users/<user>/.local/bin/common/<lever>` goes to the classifier. A leading variable assignment defeats a rule too ("An allow rule won't match past an assignment of any other variable", code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions, "Wrappers"), and so does a `perl -e 'alarm …; exec @ARGV'` wrapper, so the levers read `HCOM_DIR` and `HCOM_TEAM` from the environment and bound their own waits (`worktree-gc.sh` bounds each `gh` call by `WORKTREE_GC_GH_TIMEOUT`, default 60 seconds). A chain is never covered: "A rule must match each subcommand independently" (code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions).

`scripts/validate-agent-assets.py` (`validate_claude_permissions_allow`) refuses every other allow rule: only `Bash(hcom list *)` and `Bash(~/.local/bin/common/<name> *)` pass, and a rule naming `scripts/orchestrator/` is refused as a path inside the rewritable checkout. `<name>` needs a `claude.levers.sha256` pin, a byte-identical generated copy `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_<name>`, and a source `scripts/orchestrator/<name>` that is a regular file (not a symlink), executable, starting with `#!`, and not named as an interpreter (`sh`, `bash`, `python3`, `node`, `perl`, `env`, `uv`, `xargs` and the like, extension ignored).

The settings carry the rest of what the levers need:

- No `env` entry (U33): U16's session-wide `HCOM_DIR=~/.local/state/hcom-regime` and `HCOM_TEAM=regime` bound every project on the host to the dotfiles store. The levers now derive the store and team from the checkout they run in (`regime-env`); the seat's environment table in `docs/orchestrator-seat.md` says where each value comes from.
- `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite` gains `~/.local/state/hcom-regime` (the dotfiles program's store until its one-time move to the derived `~/.local/state/hcom/dotfiles`, README "Run a program in another repository") and `~/.local/state/hcom`, the parent of the derived stores (`claude.sandbox.filesystem.extra_allow_write`), because hcom secures the store directory on every open, `hcom list` included (inside the sandbox it failed with `Failed to secure hcom directory`), and `~/.local/state/omp-seats`, because `hcom-seat worker` creates and reuses the worker seats' worktrees there.

Invariants, held by the validator and the generator tests: no `Bash(*)`, `Bash(hcom *)`, `Bash(git *)` or `Bash(git worktree remove *)`, no interpreter or shell-wrapper prefix; every GitHub write stays in `ask` (the `gh pr` merge, create, comment, review, close, edit and update-branch rules, `gh issue`, the `gh api` write forms); the `Bash(sudo:*)` and `Bash(rm -rf:*)` denies stay; `sandbox.allowUnsandboxedCommands` stays `false` and `sandbox.excludedCommands` stays exactly `["git push *", "gh *"]`, each exclusion with its identical `Bash(...)` ask rule (U68); `autoMode.classifyAllShell` stays unset.

A lever never runs outside the sandbox. An allow rule also approves an unsandboxed retry of the command it matches ("An allow rule that matches a command also approves an unsandboxed retry of it", code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing), so the managed settings turn the retry off: `allowUnsandboxedCommands` is `false` (strict sandbox mode, where Claude Code ignores the `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` parameter), and no lever is in `sandbox.excludedCommands`, which holds only `git push *` and `gh *`, each behind its ask rule (U68). In the installed Claude Code 2.1.310, the sandbox decision returns "sandboxed" for a lever call that carries `dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true` once the setting is `false`. `hcom-seat` on macOS cannot start a seat from a Claude session, because the sandbox allows no Unix socket and the seat starts through the herdr socket; the omp orchestrator seat or a terminal starts seats.

Operator steps that stay operator steps by design: `make update` (it runs outside every sandbox and installs these settings), browser and CLI logins, anything that reads the Keychain, and, for the Claude coordinator, closing a pull request (`gh pr close`); the omp orchestrator seat closes the rows a clean-up table marks `close:` through `pr-close.sh` (section "Seat levers for GitHub writes").

### `worktree-gc.sh`

`scripts/orchestrator/worktree-gc.sh [--apply] [<path>...] | --purge <days>` reads `git worktree list --porcelain -z` and considers only the worktrees under `<toplevel>/.claude/worktrees/`, except those in its quarantine `.claude/worktrees/.gc-quarantine/`, which are never candidates (a path argument there is refused with exit 2). A worktree qualifies when `git status --porcelain --ignored=matching --untracked-files=all` prints nothing and its work landed: its head is reachable from `origin/main`; it landed by content, meaning every path `git diff --no-renames --name-only -z <merge-base> <head>` names holds in `origin/main` the `git ls-tree -r` entry (mode and object) it holds in the head, or is absent from both (a squash merge with no later commit); or its head is the `headRefOid` of a merged pull request of this repository (`gh pr list --state merged --head <branch> --json number,headRefOid --limit 100`, detached `--search <sha>`). The content test runs no merge, so a configured merge driver (`merge.default`, a `merge=` attribute) cannot resolve an unlanded conflict to `main`'s tree, and a git failure there is an `error` row that moves nothing. A merged pull request with the branch's name and another head does not count: commits pushed after a squash merge are not in `main`, and `--head` also matches a fork's branch of that name. An ignored file keeps the tree, because `git worktree remove` deletes ignored files and exits 0 (git-worktree(1) defines clean as no untracked files and no tracked modification). A locked or missing worktree is kept. It prints one row per candidate, `<gc|keep|error>\t<path>\t<branch or (detached <sha>)>\t<reason>`, one `quarantine` row per quarantined worktree with its age, then a `summary` row, and exits 0 when nothing qualifies. No check can be made atomic with a deletion (audit rounds U56b and U56c each found a file or commit written between the last check and `git worktree remove`), so taking a tree out of use and deleting it are separate runs, as Codex snapshots a managed worktree before deleting it and Claude Code deletes only after `cleanupPeriodDays`. `--apply` deletes nothing: each qualifying tree is renamed by `git worktree move <path> .gc-quarantine/<random>/<name>` (one rename on the same filesystem, so a process inside keeps its working directory; git refuses a locked tree), `<epoch> <classified head>` is written to `gc-quarantined-at` in its admin directory, and the moved tree is checked again: clean, ignored files included, and its HEAD the classified one. Both hold: a `quarantined` row; either fails (a `.env` written or a commit made while `gh` answered, say): a `held` row with the reason, and the tree stays in the quarantine with that change, never moved back. A refused move is a `failed` row and exit 1. `--purge <days>` reads only the quarantine: an entry whose marker is at least `<days>` days old (`0`: any age) is classified again by the same rules, HEAD read again, then removed with `git worktree remove <path>`, which also removes its admin data; an entry without a marker, younger, locked or no longer qualifying is kept, and a refused removal is a `failed` row and exit 1. Nothing passes `--force`, so git still refuses a tree with tracked or untracked changes, and the branch refs stay. It never runs `git worktree prune`, which would drop the admin data of every worktree whose directory is missing, an unlocked omp seat included. A path argument narrows the listing and `--apply`, and is refused with exit 2 outside `.claude/worktrees/`; `--purge` takes none. `origin/main` is read as it is, so run `git fetch origin` first. `tests/unit/test_worktree_gc.py` runs it in a scratch repository with a fake `gh` and a `git` wrapper that logs every argv.

### Measurement after landing

The coordinator measures from inside its sandbox, after the operator's `make update` and a session restart. Each command runs with no classifier call and no unsandboxed retry, and prints:

```text
$ ~/.local/bin/common/bus-history.sh dotfiles --limit 3
<id>	<at>	<from>	<to>	<kind>	<body>                     three rows, ascending by id
$ hcom list -v
◉ <seat name> now: listening                                 one block per running seat
$ ~/.local/bin/common/hcom-seat worker --dry-run
hcom omp --tag worker --dir ~/.local/state/omp-seats/dotfiles/worker --terminal herdr --go -- --profile worker --config ~/.agents/omp/policies/worker.yml --config ~/.agents/omp/profiles/worker.yml --append-system-prompt ~/.agents/omp/rules/worker.md -e ~/.local/lib/omp-extensions/pi-claude-bridge -e ~/.agents/omp/extensions/openai-siwc
```

`hcom-seat` prints the paths expanded (`/Users/<user>/...`). Paste the three outputs into the ledger row.

`core.hooksPath` hooks that lock the tree, write a marker and exit 1): `test_no_hook_of_the_checkout_runs_while_the_tree_is_made`; a required smudge filter of the checkout not running: not six listed ones) not reaching the lever's git, the checkout gaining no file: `test_an_index_file_or_object_directory_of_the_caller_does_not_reach_the_lever_s_git`; the default root under `TMPDIR`:
