| `checkout-guard.sh`, the branch moves of the coordinator's checkout | closed: with the marker `regime-coordinator` beside the hook and no store (regime-cutover U219: the trigger once the live store has left the checkout, U221), `git commit`, `commit --no-verify`, `commit --amend`, `merge --no-ff`, `cherry-pick`, `rebase`, `branch -f`, `update-ref` and a commit in a linked worktree are each refused with the route and move no branch, and the coordinator's moves pass: `test_with_the_marker_and_no_store_every_way_of_authoring_a_commit_is_refused_and_no_branch_moves`; with a store and the marker removed (the pre-move trigger, which U221 removes), the same commands and a commit in a linked worktree are each refused and move no branch (`rebase` leaves HEAD detached in its state until `rebase --abort`, pinned): `test_every_way_of_authoring_a_commit_is_refused_and_no_branch_moves`, `test_a_commit_in_a_linked_worktree_of_the_checkout_is_refused`; `pull --ff-only`, `reset --hard origin/main`, `fetch`, `branch -D` and `worktree add -b x origin/main` pass: `test_the_moves_the_coordinator_makes_pass`; `SHELLOPTS=noexec`, `BASH_ENV`, `PYTHONPATH` with a planted module and the bypass variables change nothing: `test_no_environment_variable_bypasses_the_hook`; a mode-000 or 0111 `.orchestration`, `orchestrate` or program directory refuses naming it, a later unreadable program directory after a readable store too (each is listed, in that order and to the last, never a `stat` of `units.tsv`): `test_a_store_that_cannot_be_read_refuses_the_move`; a checkout path holding a newline is guarded in the main and the linked worktree, by the store and by the marker read as bytes: `test_a_checkout_path_holding_a_newline_is_guarded`; each git read of the hook failing (a `GIT_EXEC_PATH` shim) refuses naming it: `test_a_failing_git_read_of_the_hook_refuses_the_move`; a replaced origin tip (`refs/replace/*`, `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`) does not rewrite the ancestry the hook reads: `test_a_replaced_origin_tip_does_not_rewrite_the_ancestry_the_hook_reads`; a grafted origin tip (`.git/info/grafts`, a `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` of the caller) does not either (`GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null` on every read of the hook): `test_a_grafted_origin_tip_does_not_rewrite_the_ancestry_the_hook_reads`; `pack-refs --all` and `gc` pass over a branch already off origin (the packed backend's creation line, `<zero> <value>`, for a branch that already names the commit is a backend update): `test_pack_refs_and_gc_pass_over_a_branch_that_is_already_off_origin`; the hook's stdin format pinned against the running git and the synthetic lines (deletion, tag, `ref:`, the packed creation of a branch already at the commit, a new branch off origin, malformed): `test_the_prepared_phase_feeds_old_new_ref_lines_as_githooks_documents`, `test_the_hook_judges_the_documented_lines`; without a store and without the marker the hook is inert, and `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` runs no hook: `test_without_a_store_the_hook_passes_everything`, `test_the_isolated_git_of_the_levers_is_unaffected`; a symbolic link (one resolving to the marker's bytes, or dangling) or a directory at the marker's path is no marker for the hook (a commit passes without a store, is refused with one) and is refused by `check` and `install` naming the kind with nothing written through it: `test_a_marker_that_is_a_symlink_or_a_directory_is_refused_by_install_and_ignored_by_the_hook`; install on a main checkout writes the hook (0755) and the marker (0644, its one line) and a second install prints `unchanged` for each byte for byte, round 1's hooks removed on an upgrade and a foreign file at their paths left alone: `test_install_on_a_main_checkout_writes_the_hook_and_a_second_install_is_a_no_op`, `test_install_removes_round_1_s_hooks_and_leaves_any_other_file_at_their_paths`; a checkout upgraded from the pre-move guard gets the marker alone (`unchanged` hook, `installed` marker), `check` names a missing marker `absent` (exit 1) and a changed one `differs`, which `install` refuses and never overwrites: `test_install_writes_the_marker_alone_into_a_guarded_checkout_and_check_reports_a_missing_one`; a checkout upgraded from the pre-move lever's hook (7b570792, installed by that lever rendered from `git show`, U219b) is `older` for `check` (exit 1, the marker `absent`, with or without its execute bit), `install` replaces it (`upgraded`) and writes the marker (`installed`), after which `check` is 0, a second install is `unchanged` and a commit is refused; bytes of neither version stay `differs` and refused; the hash tool failing on the hook's read (exit 7 after a plausible digest) refuses `check` and `install` naming the hook: `test_a_hook_the_pre_move_lever_wrote_is_older_and_install_upgrades_it`; the marker's own `mktemp`, `cat`, `chmod`, `cmp` and `mv` each failing with exit 7 after plausible output (the `cat` printing the marker's own line into the temporary file) make `install` exit 2 naming the step with no marker row, no temporary file, no partial marker and the hook as found: `test_a_failing_producer_of_the_marker_s_write_leaves_no_partial_marker_and_the_hook_as_found`; the hook's `lstat` of the marker failing (the hooks directory made unsearchable by the hook's own first git read, after python has read the file) refuses the commit naming the marker's path, marker present or not, and the branch stays: `test_a_marker_read_that_fails_refuses_the_move`; a linked worktree, a foreign or symlinked hook, a path outside a checkout and a usage error each refused with nothing written: `test_install_refuses_a_linked_worktree_and_a_foreign_hook_and_writes_nothing`; a failing `mktemp`, the installer's toplevel read and the `cat` that writes the expected bytes (each exit 28 on PATH) refuse both with the hooks directory unchanged: `test_a_failing_mktemp_refuses_check_and_install_and_writes_nothing`, `test_a_failing_toplevel_read_or_hook_write_refuses_check_and_install_and_writes_nothing`; a symlink, a directory or a fifo at the hook path refuses `check` and `install` naming the kind, a regular file of other content is `differs`: `test_check_refuses_to_judge_a_symlink_or_a_non_regular_file_at_the_hook_path`; a hooks directory of mode 000 refuses both naming the read, never `absent`: `test_a_hooks_directory_that_cannot_be_read_refuses_check_and_install`; the installer's git-dir and common-dir reads failing each a refusal: `test_a_failing_read_refuses_before_anything_is_written`; the install's write is one rename per file of a temporary file written, made executable (the hook) or 0644 (the marker) and read back in the hooks directory, the hook's rename first, so `mkdir`, the hook's own `cat` (the sixth of a run, after five scratch copies), `chmod`, the read-back `cmp` and the `mv` each failing (exit 28 on PATH) leave the hook path as found, and the round-1 `rm` failing after the renames leaves the hook and the marker in place: `test_a_failing_write_step_of_install_leaves_the_hook_path_as_found`; a `.git` symlink to a separate git directory whose name ends in LF is installed into, hook and marker, not its stripped sibling (each path git prints is read whole): `test_a_separate_git_directory_whose_name_ends_in_lf_is_installed_into`; a `.git` symlink to a separate git directory with a store and a linked worktree (U129's filed P1: `worktree list` named the git directory, so the linked worktree's moves passed): the linked worktree's commit and `update-ref` are refused naming the common directory, store or no store, and the main checkout's commit with the route: `test_a_linked_worktree_of_a_checkout_whose_git_directory_is_a_symlink_target_is_guarded`; the staged-file `mktemp` alone failing (the second call of a run, a counted shim) leaves an existing hooks directory's listing, bytes and modes unchanged: `test_a_failing_staged_file_mktemp_leaves_an_existing_hooks_directory_unchanged`; a hooks directory `install` made is removed again on that failure and one that existed before stays: `test_a_failed_install_removes_the_hooks_directory_it_made_and_never_one_that_existed`; `not-an-oid not-an-oid refs/heads/main`, uppercase or short hex and a bare `ref:` are each refused naming the value, a SHA-256 deletion line passes: `test_the_hook_judges_the_documented_lines`; `check` naming a hook with one NUL byte changed as `differs`: `test_check_reports_drift_and_install_repairs_only_its_own`; the caller's `GIT_DIR` naming another repository scrubbed, so the install lands in the checkout named by the path: `test_a_caller_s_git_environment_pointing_at_another_repository_does_not_move_the_install`; out of scope (unit=U129 gate=TRUST: the worker is a non-adversarial actor whose reflex the refusal stops): git resolving its hooks to another place under the caller's configuration or environment, whenever and however set, and a worker who replaces a branch with a symbolic ref or copies a branch already off origin, each a standing-order breach the records catch, not a defect the hook closes; residual: a commit on a detached HEAD moves no branch; the operator's own removal of the marker makes the guard inert by hand (the marker is removed by nothing else: the hooks directory is write-denied to every seat, hcom-seat's `git_state` rows); owner: the coordinator |

## Seat levers for GitHub writes

Regime-cutover U28. The GitHub writes the loop needs besides the merge, the worker's push and `gh pr create`/`gh pr comment` run from the omp seat whose role owns them, each through one lever below, so that no step but a login or a Keychain prompt waits for a human. Each is a `scripts/orchestrator/` source with shdoc, installed as `~/.local/bin/common/<lever>` (mode 0755; the `claude.levers.sha256` pin and the U31 self-check detect a rewritten copy, they do not prevent one, since the same user can write the copy and the manifest), and allowed by name in its role's omp policy (`home/dot_agents/omp/policies/README.md`, "GitHub-write levers (U28)"); the Claude coordinator gets none of them and keeps its `ask` rules. Common to all: `set -euo pipefail`; every argument checked before the first `gh` call; `<owner>/<repo>` from `gh repo view --json nameWithOwner`, never from an argument; each `gh` call bounded by `PR_LEVER_GH_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 60, perl `alarm`); a failed read is exit 1 and writes nothing (`preferences.md` standing order 21); a refusal is exit 2 with `<lever>: refused: <why>` on stderr and no write; the receipt is one line on stdout. No lever passes `--delete-branch`, `--admin` or `--rebase`.

The reply grammar (`preferences.md` standing order 22) is one first line: `fixed: <40-hex sha>`, `refuted: <text citing a URL or a file:line>`, `residual: unit=U<n> gate=<clause>`, `residual: operator=<decisions.tsv ts>` or `filed: f=<n> s=<n> <reason>` (regime-cutover U124). `pr-thread-reply.sh` posts only such a line. Whether it holds is judged by one function, `disposition`, the same text in `pr-thread-resolve.sh` and `pr-threads-check.sh` (`DispositionDriftTest`): `fixed:` names a local commit in the head and committed after the finding, `refuted:` cites, `residual: unit=` answers a P2 or P3 with its `units.tsv` row, `residual: operator=` has its `decisions.tsv` row, and `filed:` answers a finding of any priority when both scores match `^(10|[1-9])$` as decimal strings before any arithmetic (`score_re`, named once in each lever; a decimal past bash's range cannot wrap into a score, U124 round 2) and their product, the finding's weight, is under `WEIGHT_THRESHOLD` (10, assigned once in each lever; AGENTS.md "Audit" names the same number once and `tests/unit/test_review_rules.py` keeps them equal), refused naming the product otherwise; a `filed:` of any other shape is refused as `filed: reply needs f=<1..10> s=<1..10> <reason>; <what is wrong>; got: <the line>`, never as a bare `not a disposition`. Whatever its form, a disposition holds only when the newest reply's author is a maintainer of the repository: GitHub's `authorAssociation` of that comment is `OWNER`, `MEMBER` or `COLLABORATOR` (`maintainer_associations`, named once in each lever; U124 round 3), else the thread is `open (newest reply by <login>, association <value>: not a maintainer)` whatever the reply says; and the line is judged as the reply wrote it, the listing's `@tsv` escape of a tab decoded first, so a tab-only reason is a missing reason, not the two characters `\t` (U124 round 3). The reason is the text after `s=<n>` with the whitespace around it trimmed, so `filed: f=1 s=1  reason` (two spaces) holds, and a reply is read at its first non-blank line, one of blank lines alone left `open (the newest reply by <login>, association <value>, holds no text)`, so an outsider's reply opened by a blank line is still named by login and association (U125 round d, the two filed findings of U124 round 3). The resolve lever resolves only a thread whose disposition holds, and the merge check applies it to every thread, resolved ones included (U28b F1). In the merge gate (`pr-merge.sh` `require_dispositions`) a `dispositions.tsv` row holds for a P2 or P3 finding of the audited head's verdict file as a `residual` row whose reference names a `units.tsv` row, or, since regime-cutover U180, as a `fixed` row whose reference starts with the 40-hex sha of the head being merged while that head is carried (the audited head differs from the head being merged): the only fix that can exist at a carried head is a body fix, since a code change would have changed the digest, and the sha is the reference's first token, read as 40 hex followed by the end or a character outside hex and compared whole; a `fixed` row naming any other head, or any `fixed` row when the audited head is the head being merged, never satisfies the gate for the head it judges.

only the exact row of the sweep rule admits the merge, a hand-written row (pr `7`, verifier `coordinator`, the skill without its pin, evidence under `verdicts/`) being a malformed store to `ledger.py` (`tests.unit.test_ledger.LedgerSweepTest.test_a_sweep_row_that_is_not_the_exact_row_of_the_rule_is_malformed_naming_the_field`) and a pin word that is no prefix of the manifest's pin a refusal naming both (`test_only_the_exact_sweep_row_of_the_rule_admits_the_merge`), the lever reads the verifier and the evidence by their shape whatever the reader answered (`test_the_lever_reads_the_row_s_verifier_and_evidence_by_their_shape_whatever_the_reader_answered`), and the findings file must be a regular file of this user under `<store>/sweeps/` (`test_the_findings_file_a_sweep_row_names_must_be_a_regular_file_of_this_user_under_the_store`); the brief probe, the `briefs/` checks and their tests are gone with the class (U244); the store rule of U216 (`test_the_derived_store_is_the_store_when_it_exists_and_the_checkout_form_is_refused_then`: `--records` naming the derived store merges with the `derived` line, the checkout form refused naming both while it exists, another program's derived store not the body's program's, a symbolic link or a file at `<root>/<program>` refused by name whichever form `--records` names; `test_the_checkout_store_stands_as_the_pre_move_form_while_the_derived_store_is_absent`: the checkout form with the `pre-move form, removed by U221` line, nothing made under HOME, another program's derived store changing nothing, a `--records` outside the checkout still named as such) |

## `pr-audit.sh`: the Codex audit route

Regime-cutover U71b, operator decision G18 (2026-10-26): keep the ChatGPT login, keep the auditor from reading credentials. `pr-audit.sh <pr>` audits the pull request's current head with one fresh `codex exec` and prints the path of `verdict.md` and `Verdict: correct|incorrect|blocked`, the value read from the structured verdict (U106, below). It is installed and pinned like the levers above (`claude.levers.sha256`), run from the repository checkout in the operator's or the coordinator's terminal outside every seat (never from a seat's checkout; the run hands back its verdict path, and the orchestrator seat copies the file and writes the ledger row), and writes only inside one run directory `<root>/pr-audit-<pr>.XXXXXX`, the root being `$TMPDIR` (default `/tmp`) by its physical path, refused before anything is made there when it or an ancestor holds `.git`, `AGENTS.md` or `AGENTS.override.md` (codex would read instructions from them): a fresh repository that borrows the checkout's objects through `objects/info/alternates` and fetches `refs/pull/<pr>/head` and `main` from `origin`, so the checkout's refs and git directory are never written. The one write outside it is Codex's own: the unsandboxed Codex process refreshes a stale ChatGPT session in place through the login link, rewriting `${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}/auth.json`, as [OpenAI's CI/CD auth guide](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/auth/ci-cd-auth.md) prescribes ("Keep the refreshed `auth.json` for the next run"; one `auth.json` copy per serialized job stream). A copy in the run directory would rotate the refresh token and leave the caller's file stale; the commands the auditor runs cannot read or write the file. Every `GIT_*` variable the caller exports is removed before any git runs (U71b audit round 7: an inherited `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` made the audited range empty), and the fresh repository must hold no grafts or shallow file. The head is checked out by a git that reads no global or system config or attributes file, so a `.gitattributes` in the pull request cannot start a filter driver the caller installed (U71b audit round 1). The fetched head must equal `gh pr view`'s `headRefOid`, else the run is refused (the head moved). The prompt holds the `## Audit` section of the fetched `main:AGENTS.md` (the blob read once, its size checked, the section cut out by the lever in one pass; a failed or short read, a missing or doubled heading or an empty section refuses before codex starts), the pull request body (its `## Contract` section is the contract; a body without one is refused) as data inside a per-run nonce tag, and the range `<merge-base>..<head>`. Every `codex` call runs in a process group of its own; on `PR_AUDIT_TIMEOUT` the whole group is stopped (the `codex` launcher starts a native child that otherwise kept auditing after the deadline). Exit 0: the verdict is written; 1: a read, the fetch or the run failed, codex wrote no final message, or the verdict file is not valid JSON, does not conform to the schema or contradicts its findings; 2: refused, nothing audited. The run keeps `prompt.md`, `verdict.schema.json`, `codex.log`, `verdict.json` and `verdict.md`; the scratch repository, HOME, CODEX_HOME and TMPDIR are removed. `--cyber-program <standard|daybreak_blue>` (U413) names the cyber access program of the audit turn: `standard` (the default, every audit since U407) is the argv as before, and `daybreak_blue` runs the turn with `--cyber-access-program daybreak_blue -c model="gpt-6-sol"` in its place, for a unit whose content the standard program's classifier refuses (measured on U343, 2026-11-09), which needs the operator's Codex session to carry the account's hardware key and changes no probe, permission profile or template.

The verdict is structured data, never a free-text last line (regime-cutover U106, plan P-24). The lever writes a JSON Schema to the run directory and passes it with `--output-schema` (codex-cli 0.164.0; `-o` keeps naming the file the final message goes to), so codex's final message is an object with `verdict` (`correct`, `incorrect` or `blocked`), `findings` (each with `priority` P0 to P3, `confidence` high, medium or low, `path`, `line` as an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (the exact line the rules ask for, never null), and `rationale`), `reproduced` (strings) and `notes` (a string or null; required and nullable, since Codex sends the schema with strict validation, under which no property is optional). The prompt tells the auditor that the rules' closing `Verdict:` line is met by the `verdict` field, from which the lever writes that line. A reader (`python3 -I -S`, status checked) validates `verdict.json` against the same schema file and refuses (exit 1, naming the file) invalid JSON, a missing or unknown key, a value outside its enum, type or bound, or a finding without its keys. It reads JSON as RFC 8259 defines it, not as Python's json module extends it: `NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity` are not JSON values, a key written twice in one object is refused rather than read as its last value, every number is read as an exact decimal and one outside what that represents is refused rather than raising, and a string holding a lone surrogate is not of type string. It refuses the schema itself when it uses a keyword or type the reader does not implement, so no rule binds codex alone. The decision rule is applied from the data: any P0 or P1 finding means `incorrect`, none means `correct` (or `blocked`); a P2 or P3 finding is reported with its priority and confidence and changes no verdict (regime-cutover U119, decision 2026-10-30 change 1: the thread rule of `pr-threads-check.sh` closes it as `fixed:` or `residual: unit=U<n> gate=<clause>`); a verdict that contradicts its findings, or a priority outside P0 to P3, is refused naming the field, never corrected. The reader then writes `verdict.md` (the findings as a list, what was reproduced, the notes, then `Blocking: <count of P0 and P1 findings>` and the one last line `Verdict: <value>`) whole or not at all (under a partial name, renamed over the final one), the file whose path the lever prints, so the store's `verdicts/U<n>-<sha8>.md` copy keeps its shape. `--ignore-rules` is passed (no execpolicy `.rules` file of the caller or the head is loaded); `--ignore-user-config` is not, since it would skip the scratch `config.toml` that selects the audit profile.

Codex runs with a scratch HOME and a scratch CODEX_HOME holding `auth.json`, a symlink to `${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}/auth.json` (refused unless that is a regular file), and `config.toml`, written from `home/dot_agents/codex-audit/config.toml` (installed as `~/.agents/codex-audit/config.toml` beside the manifest the lever's self-check reads). The generator renders that file from `codex.audit` in the manifest: the `review` profile's Codex model pin, skills and web search off, project instructions off (`project_doc_max_bytes = 0`, `project_doc_fallback_filenames = []`, `project_root_markers = []`, each required once before the first table; the rules arrive in the prompt), `shell_environment_policy` `inherit = "core"`, and the [permission profile](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/permissions) `audit` as `default_permissions`. It extends `:read-only`, disables the network, and its filesystem is an allowlist (U71b audit round 4: a deny list misses every store a caller keeps elsewhere, such as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` or an XDG directory): `":root" = "deny"`, then read for `:minimal` (Codex's platform and runtime set), the head's tree, the empty cwd, `/opt/homebrew` (git; `/usr/bin/git` is an xcrun shim that needs a writable cache, and mise's python links its libraries) and `~/.local/share/mise/installs` (python3, uv, shellcheck, shfmt, ruff on PATH), write for `:tmpdir` only (the run's scratch TMPDIR, so unit tests can make temp files), and deny for `/tmp` (`:minimal` reads it), `/var/tmp` (`:minimal` makes it writable) and the credential stores again. The Xcode command line tools, the mise shims and the uv cache are not needed (measured). The lever replaces each path key with absolute paths (`~/<path>` under the real HOME, since Codex's own `~` is the scratch HOME, `/<path>`, `$AUDIT_TREE`, `$AUDIT_CWD`, `$CODEX_HOME`, `$CODEX_LOGIN_HOME`, each also in its `pwd -P` spelling, plus the login file's resolved path) and refuses a config that does not make `audit` the default, names `sandbox_mode`, does not deny `:root`, makes anything but `:tmpdir` writable, or resolves one path to two accesses. The fetched objects are copied into the run's repository (`git repack -a -d`) and the borrowing is dropped, so the checkout's object store stays outside the allowlist. A profile does not compose with `--sandbox`/`sandbox_mode`, so the lever never passes `-s`.

Before the audit, `codex sandbox -- /bin/sh -c …` runs twice with the same scratch CODEX_HOME and the default profile. The first must find unreadable every denied path that exists (the login, its resolved file, the scratch link, each deny path), a canary the lever writes in the run directory outside the allowlist, the real HOME, and every store the caller's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and XDG base-directory variables name (`~` resolved under the real HOME, a relative path against the caller's cwd, then by physical path; a store inside an allowed read or write root refuses the run, since the allowlist would expose it), and must fail to make a directory in the run directory and in each directory an absolute deny key names (`/var/tmp`). A denial counts only when the tool ran and its error names it (`Operation not permitted`, `Permission denied`, `Read-only file system`); a tool that could not run (exit 42, 126, 127 without that text) is an unproven denial and refuses. Positive controls run first in the same sandbox: `cat` of a file and `ls` of the head's tree and `mkdir` in the scratch TMPDIR must succeed. The second needs `curl https://github.com` to exist and exit 6 (resolve failure) or 7 (connect failure); any other status (0, a timeout, 126/127), no curl, or a probe that cannot run is a refusal. Every `codex` call runs in a session and process group of its own under one Python supervisor, embedded in the lever and run as `python3 -I -S` (isolated; regime-cutover U71b audit round 12 replaced a hand-built perl supervisor whose failure paths opened a new window in four rounds). Its INT, TERM and HUP handlers, installed before `subprocess.Popen(…, start_new_session=True)`, only record the signal (round 13: a handler that waited re-entered `Popen.wait` under its lock and deadlocked); the main flow alone does process work, waiting in slices of at most 0.2 s and checking the recorded signal and the deadline after each. Everything after the launch runs in `try`/`finally`, and the `finally` always runs the stop: a fixed list of steps (ignore signals, `ps` snapshot, `killpg` TERM, a 5 s grace, `killpg` KILL, reap, the Linux subreaper sweep, the survivor report) run by one runner in which each step has its own `try`/`except` and the next always runs (round 15), so KILL and the reap run even when every earlier step raised and no exit path leaves the group running; a step that raised makes the exit 125. The exit is decided once after the wait: a recorded signal gives 128 + its number, else a passed deadline gives 124 (also for a wait that returned late), else codex's status. Process groups are no containment boundary (round 14): the deadline stops codex's process group on both OSes and every descendant on Linux, where the supervisor is the child subreaper (`prctl(PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER)` before the launch, a failure refuses) and KILLs and reaps every child it inherits until none is left, bounded by the grace; on macOS a descendant that starts its own session can outlive the run, confined by the Codex sandbox profile it inherited (no writes outside the scratch, no network, credential stores denied), and the lever prints a warning naming every process (pid, command) a `ps` snapshot taken just before the cleanup saw under the supervisor and that is still alive after it (detection only: a process that double-forked away before the snapshot is not seen; a failed `ps` is a failed stop step, exit 125); a failed launch, a `killpg` failure other than ESRCH, a failed reap and any other exception mark the reasons and exit 125, which the lever refuses. The supervisor stops codex by itself when the lever goes away (rounds 16 and 17): the lever passes its own pid; the supervisor launches nothing unless its parent is that pid, checked before the launch and on Linux again right after asking for SIGTERM on the parent's death (`prctl(PR_SET_PDEATHSIG)`, a failure refuses), and on both OSes treats a parent other than that pid in any wait slice as a SIGTERM, so a KILL to the lever still stops codex within the grace. The lever removes every exported `GIT_*`, `PERL*` and `PYTHON*` variable as its first statements, before any external command (round 17: the self-check's `shasum` fallback is a perl script). The lever runs as `/bin/bash -p` (its shebang), so no exported shell function (`BASH_FUNC_jobs%%`, `BASH_FUNC_command%%`) and no BASH_ENV, SHELLOPTS, BASHOPTS, CDPATH or GLOBIGNORE reaches it; a run through another shell is refused. The lever forwards INT, TERM and HUP to the supervisor, which it finds in its job table (a failed lookup is reported as such: the supervisor then stops itself when the lever exits), and exits 128 + the signal's number; its run directory's creation until the cleanup trap is set is a held window (a signal there is acted on when it closes). Measured on macOS with codex-cli 0.163.0 and this profile: `git log`, `python3 -c`, `bash -n`, shellcheck and `python3 -m unittest tests.unit.test_lint_retired_terms` run in the tree; a canary under a fake `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (in `$TMPDIR` and in `/tmp`), `~`, `~/.config`, `~/.local/share`, the checkout's `.git` and the login are `Operation not permitted`; `/var/tmp` and the tree refuse writes; curl exits 6. Linux is not measured; there the probes are the guard.

```sh
~/.local/bin/common/pr-audit.sh 547
```

| Control                                                      | Disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `scripts/orchestrator/pr-audit.sh`                           | closed: `tests.unit.test_pr_audit.PrAuditTest`: exact `codex exec` argv without `-s`, scratch HOME/CODEX_HOME/TMPDIR, rules from `main` only, nothing written outside the run directory; refusals with no `codex exec` for a config without the `audit` default, `sandbox_mode`, a foreign deny key, a missing or non-regular login, a template read that fails midway, a probe that reads a denied path, a curl status other than 6 or 7, a `.gitattributes` filter driver (must not run), a moved head, a base other than `main`, no `## Contract`; `test_the_decision_rule_is_applied_from_the_findings` (U119: a `correct` with a P2 passes with `Blocking: 0`, a P1 is `incorrect` with `Blocking: 1`, a verdict its findings do not derive or a priority outside P0 to P3 is exit 1 naming the field, no `verdict.md`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `home/dot_agents/codex-audit/config.toml` and `codex.audit`  | closed: `test_the_config_is_the_generated_source_with_every_path_resolved` (the run's config is the generated allowlist with each path key resolved, both spellings of HOME); `make render-check` keeps the file equal to the manifest                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| the auditor's own process                                    | residual: the profile binds the commands codex runs, not codex itself, which reads the login through the link to authenticate and rewrites it when it refreshes the session (the documented in-place refresh; `test_codex_refreshing_its_login_writes_the_callers_auth_json_and_nothing_else`); the launch-time probes measure the commands' side on every run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `codex sandbox` and `codex exec` resolving the profile alike | residual: the probes run through `codex sandbox`; the lever checks the one config both read selects `audit` and names no `sandbox_mode`; a codex release that resolved them differently would need a new measurement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| the round cap (standing order 36, U120)                      | closed: a `Program-Unit:` body needs `--records <store>`, and the checkout's `scripts/orchestrator/rounds-check.py <store> U<n> --pr <pr>` runs before anything else over the body's unit and every unit the ledger names for the pull request (a `U<n>b` fix unit keeps its own id in its rows, standing order 24); a unit's rounds are counted since its last merge, because a merge ends a change and a new pull request does not (U133; round 2 after Codex thread 4437730129 on PR 623): the check counts the unit's audit rows after its latest `pr-merge.sh` ledger row, whatever pull request each names, a `-` cell and a merge row are never audit rows and the mechanism rows stay the unit's, so three `correct` records syncs each merged on its own row are no rounds of one change (the cap that refused syncs 16 and 20) while a change moved unmerged to a new pull request keeps its count (`--all` judges every unit the ledger names, one line each): `test_a_program_pull_request_is_audited_only_with_records_the_round_cap_allows` (three incorrect rows without a mechanism-change row are exit 3 with the reason, no codex, nothing left), `test_a_fix_unit_s_rows_gate_the_parent_unit_s_pull_request` (three incorrect `U7b` rows refuse a body naming `U7`), `test_a_round_cap_check_that_cannot_run_or_read_its_store_refuses_the_audit` (a check that cannot run or read its store is exit 3) and `test_a_store_that_cannot_be_entered_refuses_before_any_call` (a store `cd` refuses is exit 2, nothing left); the rule: `tests.unit.test_rounds_check` (`test_two_rounds_on_one_pull_request_then_two_on_another_are_four_rounds_of_one_change`, the Codex scenario; `test_three_correct_rows_each_followed_by_its_merge_row_allow_a_fourth`, the records unit) |
| the brief gate (standing order 37, U123)                     | closed: after the round cap, the unit's brief is the one file `<store>/briefs/U<n>-*.md` and the checkout's `scripts/orchestrator/brief-check.py <brief>` must exit 0 before anything else runs; a brief it refuses, zero or two files on the glob, a python that fails on the check or no check in the checkout is exit 3 with the check's lines on stderr, no codex, nothing left (every unit audited from here on has a brief written after U121, so no date exemption): `test_a_program_pull_request_whose_brief_fails_brief_check_is_refused_before_any_model_runs`; the rule: `tests.unit.test_brief_check`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| the earlier findings as data (standing order 38, U122)       | closed: with `--records`, the checkout's `scripts/orchestrator/audit-carry.py --render` renders the block from the store before the repack into a file of the run and the prompt carries it after the body inside the run's `<carried-<nonce>>` tags (round 3); `test_the_prompt_carries_the_earlier_findings_and_their_dispositions_as_data` (the rows from fixture files; the head on a branch from the base, so the earlier head is no ancestor and the two-dot ranges are still rendered from the borrowed objects; no block without an earlier audited head or without a `Program-Unit:` line) and `test_a_store_whose_earlier_findings_cannot_be_read_as_data_refuses_the_audit` (a missing verdict file, a malformed `dispositions.tsv` row or a missing lever is exit 2, no codex, nothing left); the reader: `tests.unit.test_audit_carry`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

## `audit-carry.py`: the earlier findings as data and the carried head

Regime-cutover U122 (decision 2026-10-30 change 3, standing order 38, gate G-20). A fresh audit of head N+1 knew nothing of head N: it re-raised findings the coordinator had closed as `residual:` (U109 rounds 21 to 23, U112 rounds 19 and 20) and audited a head whose diff against `main` was byte-identical to the last audited head's (U119 1b9af93e, a prettier-only commit). `scripts/orchestrator/audit-carry.py` is one strict reader of the store (`ledger.tsv`, `verdicts/`, `dispositions.tsv`: each row with its header's column count, a store ts and no empty required column (`ts unit pr head_sha verdict` of a ledger row), the leading `## Findings` section one `- none` line or one or more lines in the reader's scored form `- P<n> (<confidence>) [f<n> s<n>] <path>:<line>: <rationale>` (the grammar `pr-merge.sh` reads by, stated once in each; the frequency and severity scores, each one of 1 to 10, are read by value and carried as data, never second-guessed; an empty section, `- none` beside a finding, a line without the `[f<n> s<n>]` token or a score outside 1 to 10 is a malformed verdict; U125 round c), a disposition `residual` or `fixed`) behind two forms, run from the checkout as `rounds-check.py` is; it reads nothing from a pull request, and a store file it cannot read as data is exit 2 naming the file and line, never "no earlier findings" (standing order 21), and so is a `ledger.py` beside the lever that the import cannot read (U125 round c, the U122 round 6 residual: a mode-000 copy had raised out of the lever as exit 1, which `pr-audit.sh` reads as no earlier audited head). The data shape comes first: `CarriedFinding` is `priority f s path line disposition reference` (`f` and `s` the earlier audit's two scores, U125), one row per finding of the earlier audited head's verdict file (`verdicts/<row unit>-<sha8>.md`, named by the ledger row's unit, so a `U<n>b` fix unit's verdict is read under `U<n>b`) and per `dispositions.tsv` row of it (`none` and `-` for a finding without a row); `ChangedRange` is `path: start-end`, the new side of each hunk of the `--unified=0` patch between the two heads, or `path: whole file` for a changed path whose patch has no hunk (a binary file, a text file holding a NUL byte) or whose record is a typechange (`T`, a file replaced by a symlink or back: two patch sections for one record, which the one-per-record count refused as exit 2 until round 5c), every path taken from the `-z` raw records of `git diff-tree` and never from a patch header (round 4), and since round 6 each record's patch read on its own, `git diff-tree -p --unified=0` between two one-entry trees `git mktree --batch` writes from the record's two sides, so no header is rebuilt from a path either (round 6 P2: the rebuilt header octal-escaped a byte outside UTF-8 that git, under `core.quotePath=false`, had written as it was, and a path holding 0xff was exit 2; `0-0` a deleted file; a path outside ASCII is named as it is); `CarriedTable.render()` writes the one block, plain text with a tab-separated table and every path in git's C-quoted form (a quote, a backslash or a control character such as a newline makes the path `"..."` on one line); the lever fences nothing, and `pr-audit.sh` wraps the block in its own per-run nonce tags as it wraps the pull request body (round 3). What the lever reuses and does not own (round 3, the mechanism-change row of 2026-10-30T14:27:50Z): every git command runs through `git-isolated.sh` beside it, sourced by `/bin/bash -p` after its sha256 self-check, under `git_env_scrub` and `git_isolated` (`--no-replace-objects` among the library's flags and again among the lever's own, so a `refs/replace` of the head cannot stand in for an audited tree); a finding joins a `dispositions.tsv` row by the merge gate's key (unit, head_sha, priority, path, line); and every integer it reads (a finding's line, a disposition row's line, a hunk's start and count) goes through one reader that accepts 1 to 7 decimal digits without a leading zero and refuses anything else as exit 2 naming the file and line. Where git runs (round 4): the lever reads the checkout root (captured behind a sentinel, so a root whose name ends in a newline is kept whole; round 6 P3) and, for the carry decision, the base ref's sha in the coordinator's checkout and nothing else there; it makes a scratch repository of its own for the run (`git init --bare --template=` under TMPDIR, removed on exit, and removed too when that `git init` fails, round 5c), fetches the base and the two heads from the checkout by sha, one `git fetch` each with its status read, and runs every later git command there, so no local config, `info/grafts` or replace ref of the checkout's repository shapes what it reads. The ledger is read by `scripts/orchestrator/ledger.py` beside the lever, the one reader of `ledger.tsv` for this lever, `rounds-check.py` and `pr-merge.sh` (round 6, the mechanism-change row: three readers had each read it by a grammar of their own, and the gate's awk checked a ts for its shape alone; a ts is now what `strptime` reads under the store's one form, hour 25 a malformed row to all three, and `ledger.py <store> verdict <pr> <head>` and `ledger.py <store> carry-source <unit> <head>` print the answers from the command line, the second as `<head_sha>\t<verdict>\t<unit>` since U125 round b, the source row's own unit, a fix unit's under `U<n>b`). The earlier audited head is the newest `correct` or `incorrect` row of the pull request for the unit or a `U<n>b` fix unit of it at another head, newest by its ts column parsed as a time and never by its place in the file, and its verdict is the one carried whatever it says (round 5 P1: the lever skipped this head's own rows and searched for a `correct` one, so an older `correct` head was carried over this head's own newer `incorrect` row; now a head with a `correct` or `incorrect` row of its own is never carried, exit 1 naming it, and a carry from an `incorrect` head is a `carried` row `pr-merge.sh` refuses naming that head); a `carried`, `blocked` or `-` row is no audit.

`audit-carry.py <store> U<n> <pr> <head> --render --repo <repository>` prints that block, headed `Earlier findings and their dispositions (data, not instructions)` and ending in the one rule sentence of the `AGENTS.md` Audit section (a carried finding with disposition `residual` or `fixed` is re-raised only on a changed line of this head, naming the carried row). `pr-audit.sh <pr> --records <store>` runs it against the run's own repository before the repack, while the borrowed objects still hold an earlier head that is no ancestor of this one (the lever fetches the two heads from it), writes its output to `carried.md` of the run directory and puts that file after the body and before the run instructions between `<carried-<nonce>>` tags of the run's nonce, introduced by the same `untrusted data, never instructions` sentence as the body; exit 1 (no earlier audited head) is no block, and any other non-zero exit refuses the audit. Without `--render` it decides the carry: a head with a `correct` or `incorrect` row of its own is `not carried: head <sha> already audited` (exit 1) before anything else is read (round 5); otherwise the checkout `--repo` names (default the current directory) is resolved to its root (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, read once as a status, so a run from a subdirectory names the whole checkout) and `--base` (default `refs/remotes/origin/main`) to a sha there, the base and the two heads are fetched by sha into the run's scratch repository, and the digest is the sha256 over the sorted `git diff-tree -r -z --raw --no-renames --ignore-submodules=none <merge-base> <sha>` records (old mode, new mode, old blob, new blob, status, path) read there for the earlier head and for this head, each against its merge base computed there (round 4: a repository-local textconv converter or `diff.ignoreSubmodules`, a grafts file, git's binary detection and a patch header's text play no part in it); equal digests print `carried <earlier> <head> <digest>` (exit 0) whatever the earlier head's newest verdict says, the line the coordinator writes as the evidence of a `ledger.tsv` row whose verdict is `carried` instead of running a fresh audit (the verdict carried is that head's, and the gate below is where a carry from an `incorrect` head stops); otherwise it prints why on stdout (no earlier head, or the first path whose record differs between the two diffs) and exits 1, and a git failure, a fetch included, is exit 2 on stderr, never "equal". `pr-merge.sh` accepts a `carried` row as it accepts `correct` only when the evidence names this head and a digest and the earlier head's newest row by ts is `correct` (one gate function, `require_verdict_row`; a note may follow the digest after a space, `;`, `,`, `.` or `)`, so the receipt form `carried <earlier> <head> <digest>; verdicts/U<n>-<head8>.md` is admitted and a 65th hex-like character is not, U180); it then reads the earlier head's verdict file (the earlier row's unit names it) and dispositions, and CI must be green on the new head as for every head.

```sh
uv run --no-project python -I scripts/orchestrator/audit-carry.py .orchestration/orchestrate/regime-cutover U122 601 <head>
uv run --no-project python -I scripts/orchestrator/filed-findings.py .orchestration/orchestrate/regime-cutover
```
