## `git-isolated.sh`: one isolated git for the levers

Regime-cutover U113 (plan P-22, research 01 section 1). Git has no single switch that removes all configuration from a command, and until U113 four hand-made wrappers (`git_plain`, `git_scratch`, `git_safe`, `git_tree`) each carried a flag set of their own. `scripts/orchestrator/git-isolated.sh` holds the set once, as data, in two plain arrays that are never exported, and is sourced by `records-sync.sh`, `pr-audit.sh`, `pr-merge.sh`, `pr-threads-check.sh`, `pr-body-edit.sh` and `pr-thread-resolve.sh` (whose `disposition` is one text with `pr-threads-check.sh`'s, U28b F1) from the directory they run from (`LEVER_DIR`), pinned and self-checked like `regime-env`, so a rewritten copy under `~/.local/bin/common` stops the lever that sources it; `audit-carry.py` (U122 round 3) runs every git command through it as well, from a `/bin/bash -p` that sources the library beside the lever and calls `git_env_scrub` and `git_isolated`. `scripts/lint-comparisons.py` reads the same two arrays from the file's syntax tree for its own git, never by sourcing the file.

`GIT_ISOLATED_ENV`, the environment of every isolated git:

- `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`: no global config file, so no `url.*.insteadOf`, credential helper, hook path, identity or include of the operator's applies.
- `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`: no system config file.
- `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`: no system attributes file, so no filter driver comes from it.
- `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`: no credential or passphrase prompt; a transport that needs one fails instead of waiting.

`GIT_ISOLATED_ARGS`, before the caller's arguments; a caller's later `-c` wins for its key by git's last-wins rule, so `records-sync.sh` adds `credential.helper=!gh auth git-credential` after the empty helper and the tests re-open the local transport with `-c protocol.file.allow=always`:

- `--no-replace-objects`: no replacement ref stands in for an object.
- `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`: no hook of any repository runs.
- `-c core.fsmonitor=false`: no fsmonitor program (one ran inside `git ls-files`).
- `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`: no user attributes file, so no filter, diff or merge driver comes from it.
- `-c core.commitGraph=false`: no commit-graph file of the repository under an ancestry read.
- `-c core.sshCommand=false`: no ssh transport, whatever any config names.
- `-c protocol.allow=never` and `-c protocol.https.allow=always`: https is the only transport, so an `insteadOf` rewrite to a path or another scheme cannot be followed.
- `-c credential.helper=` and `-c credential.interactive=false`: no helper of any config answers, no prompt.
- `-c gc.auto=0` and `-c maintenance.auto=false`: no gc and no detached maintenance process behind a command.

`git_isolated <git arguments...>` runs git with exactly that environment and those arguments first; its body is the one place in the levers where a bare `git` word is accepted. `git_env_scrub [name to keep...]` removes every exported `GIT_*` name (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` and `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` override every file, and only this closes them) and the transport names `http_proxy`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `https_proxy`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `no_proxy`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR` and `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, except the names given as arguments, then lists again and exits 2 naming a survivor, a name it cannot unset or a listing that failed; it reads `compgen -e` through a command substitution, so bash 3.2 with an unwritable `TMPDIR` cannot skip it. `pr-audit.sh` keeps its own earlier scrub, which also removes `PERL*` and `PYTHON*` before the self-check's first external command, and does not call it.

Two bare `git` lines remain in the five levers, each on a line carrying `# git-isolated: allow <reason>`: the `read_config git user.name` and `user.email` reads of `records-sync.sh` (the identity is read from the user configuration on purpose) and the `bounded … env "${GIT_ISOLATED_ENV[@]}" git "${GIT_ISOLATED_ARGS[@]}" … fetch` of `pr-audit.sh` (a perl exec cannot run a shell function, so the fetch carries the library's own arrays).

`scripts/ci/git-invocation-check.sh` is the lint. It runs `scripts/ci/shfmt_json_contract.py` first (U105), parses each file with `shfmt -ln bash --to-json` and reports every word that is one plain literal `git` or a path ending in `/git`, wherever it stands under a statement: a command word, an argument after `command`, `exec`, `env`, `bounded` or any other wrapper, an assignment value, an array element, a redirect target, a `$(git …)` in an unquoted heredoc. The rule is structural, not a match on the first word. A word is accepted only inside the library's own `git_isolated` (the file is recognised by identity, so a copy elsewhere is reported) or when its statement spans a line carrying `# git-isolated: allow <reason>`; an allow comment without a reason is itself a finding. A quoted `'git'` or `"git …"` is text, not a plain literal, and a heredoc body is one line-shaped literal, so `refuse 'git ls-files failed'` passes. An unparsable or unreadable file, a missing shfmt, a contract mismatch or a walk that saw no command and no literal in the contract fixtures is exit 2, never a clean list. The enforced set is a test, `tests/unit/test_git_invocation_check.py`: the library and the six levers above exit 0 with no output, and the only allow lines are the two above. `hcom-seat`, `worktree-gc.sh`, `omp-run.sh`, `regime-env`, `regime-init`, `ci-rerun.sh`, `pr-thread-reply.sh` and `pr-close.sh` keep their own git until a follow-up unit; the lint reports them when run over them. `tests/unit/test_git_isolated.py` is the acceptance test of research 01 section 1.6: under a hostile repository and user configuration (every hook, an fsmonitor program, `insteadOf` and `pushInsteadOf`, `author.name`, an `includeIf`, a clean filter) the isolated `ls-files`, `hash-object`, `commit-tree`, `update-ref` and push run no hook, rewrite no URL, apply no filter and take the identity from the environment, under PATH's bash and under `/bin/bash`; a rewrite in the repository's own config is the one level no flag closes, and the same test shows a push through `--git-dir` from a repository the caller created, the shape of `records-sync.sh`, is not reached by it.

## regime-init and regime-env: the regime in any repository

Regime-cutover U33. The levers no longer assume the dotfiles checkout: they run from `~/.local/bin/common` (U31) and take the program's store, team and seat worktrees from the repository they run in. `regime-env` is the one implementation of that derivation. It is a helper the levers source from the directory they run from (`LEVER_DIR`), pinned and self-checked like a lever but never run, so it has no allow rule. It defines `regime_toplevel`, `regime_slug` and `regime_env`.

| Value               | Default when the environment does not set it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| toplevel            | the main checkout: the parent of the git common directory, so a seat worktree or a `.claude/worktrees/*` worktree derives the same values as the checkout it was added from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `<slug>`            | the toplevel's basename, lower-cased, every character outside `[a-z0-9_-]` replaced by `-`, at most 40 characters, refused unless it starts with `[a-z0-9]` (hcom's team and tag grammar)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `HCOM_TEAM`         | `<slug>`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `HCOM_DIR`          | `~/.local/state/hcom/<slug>`, bound to the checkout by its `origin` file: only `regime-init` writes it, and every other lever refuses a derived store without it or with another toplevel in it (two checkouts with one basename). No part of the path (`~/.local/state/hcom`, the store, `origin`) may be a symlink                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| seats root          | `~/.local/state/omp-seats/<slug>` (`hcom-seat --seats-root` overrides)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `REGIME_STORE_ROOT` | `<physical HOME>/.local/state/regime-store/<slug>` (U215c, handoff/07-store-move-design.md, the mechanism-change row of 2026-11-07T18:05:48Z), the root the program stores lie under (`<root>/<program>`): a fixed derivation from HOME resolved by `pwd -P` and from the slug, exported for a derived and for an explicit `HCOM_DIR` alike, never read from the environment, chosen by no binding file, option or environment value, so nothing a seat writes enters it; empty where there is no slug (outside a checkout, with an explicit `HCOM_DIR`). Every component from `.local` down is held by its own test under a parent this user can search: absent, or a real directory of this user that is no symlink and that this user can search; a link, a file, another user's directory or a directory this user cannot search is refused naming it, never read as absent. A refusal of the helper is its return 1, which each caller maps to its own status: `regime-init` exit 2, `hcom-dispatch` and `bus-history.sh` exit 1 |

An explicit `HCOM_DIR`, `HCOM_TEAM` or `--seats-root` always wins, which is how the dotfiles seats launched before U33 keep their store until the orchestrator moves them. `hcom-seat` exports the derived pair to the seat, so the seat and its stop gate read the same store; `--dry-run` prints the pair on stderr. `hcom-dispatch` and `bus-history.sh` derive the same store and refuse while its `hcom.db` does not exist. A repository whose `refs/remotes/origin/main` is absent seats its worktrees at `HEAD`; a ref that is broken or names no commit, or a failing `git for-each-ref`, is a refusal. hcom does not pass `HCOM_TEAM` into the pane it launches, so `hcom-seat` ends the launch line with `--hcom-team <team>`, exactly the team it was given, and loads the stop gate's directory `~/.agents/omp/hooks` on every launch (in `claude.levers.extensions`, U33c); the gate reads the flag, never a team derived from the cwd, and exports it as `HCOM_TEAM` into the seat.

`regime-init <program> [--force] [--write-agents]`, run in the checkout, creates the program store at `<store root>/<program>/` (regime-cutover U215, U215b and U215c, handoff/07-store-move-design.md: the store root is `regime-env`'s fixed derivation `<physical HOME>/.local/state/regime-store/<slug>`, exported as `REGIME_STORE_ROOT` (the table above), a directory outside every checkout that the orchestrator seat alone writes and the worker reads (U219: the root in both omp roles' `allowRead` and in the orchestrator's `allowWrite`, in no worker write grant; U219b: its parent `~/.local/state/regime-store` whole in `hcom-seat`'s protected set from the lever's start, like `~/.local/state/hcom` and `~/.local/state/omp-seats`, so an executable a seat plants under the root never runs on the host; U219c: every other row denies it for write, every rendered write grant is judged against it, and a launch that finds it absent makes it 0700 before its settings file is written, the paragraph "Worker reads of the coordinator's records" above), outside every worker's write grant because `hcom-seat` grants the hcom store `HCOM_DIR` itself to every seat for the bus, so round 1's root `${HCOM_DIR}/store` would have put the records inside a worker's write root (verdict U215-11a32ed6 P1) and round 2's binding file beside `origin` could have been rewritten by a seat to the same effect (verdict U215-a081d8ba P1; the mechanism-change row of 2026-11-07T18:05:48Z, form remove: no binding file, option or environment value chooses the root); `regime-init` makes `regime-store`, `<slug>` and `<program>` each by its own `mkdir` under `umask 077` and holds each one found in place to a real directory of this user whose mode is 0700, read by `/usr/bin/stat` with its status, so any other mode, a planted link or a file is refused by name before anything is written and a pre-made 0777 component is never kept (P2); a failing template copy refuses naming the file and leaves no partial store; nothing is created under the checkout, and nothing under the hcom store but `origin`; the `created` and `unchanged` rows name the root) with the templates from `~/.agents/regime/` (`preferences.md` with the host-independent standing orders, and `units.tsv`, `ledger.tsv`, `decisions.tsv`, `gates.md` with their header rows). The live store of this program, `.orchestration/orchestrate/regime-cutover/` inside the main checkout, stays where it is until U221 moves it to that root; the later store-move units (U216 `pr-merge.sh` and `pr-close.sh`, U219 `checkout-guard.sh` and `hcom-seat`, where the root enters both roles' `allowRead` and the orchestrator's write roots and the guard's trigger becomes the marker beside its hook, U220 `records-sync.sh`, U221 `regime-start`) derive the same root through `regime-env`. It also writes `~/.local/state/hcom/<slug>/origin` with the toplevel, runs `checkout-guard.sh install <toplevel>` (U129: the checkout's `reference-transaction` hook and, U219, the marker `regime-coordinator` beside it, `checkout-guard: installed` for each on the first run and `unchanged` after, round 1's `pre-commit` and `post-checkout` `removed` when still there; the guard's refusals, a linked worktree, a foreign hook or marker, are this run's; a change is the `installed` or `removed` receipt row read at line start, never the word anywhere; the marker is the hook's trigger, so a commit in the prepared checkout is refused with the route to a worker tree from the first run on, wherever the records live), then prints the AGENTS.md audit section, or appends it with `--write-agents` when `AGENTS.md` has no `## Audit` heading, and prints the next commands. A file equal to its template is left alone, one that differs is kept unless `--force` replaces it, and a second run prints `nothing changed`. It never writes through a symlink, and it refuses outside a checkout and on a slug collision.

`regime-start [<program>]` (regime-cutover U221, store move 5 of 5) chooses a program's store by the same two-form rule (`store_resolve`, in its own body): `${REGIME_STORE_ROOT}/<program>` when that directory exists, held a directory of this user and no symlink (a link or a file there is refused by name before anything starts), else the checkout's own `.orchestration/orchestrate/<program>`, the pre-move form that stands until `regime-store-move` moves the live store; the form chosen is printed once on stderr (`regime-start: store: derived <dir>`, or `regime-start: store: checkout <dir> (pre-move form, removed by U221)`), with neither the refusal names both and nothing starts, and the seat's "the store is" line names the store chosen. The hcom store and the team are chosen as before (an explicit pair, the dotfiles pre-move pair, or `regime-env`'s derivation), so a seat started on either form of the program store talks on the same bus.

`regime-store-move <program>` (regime-cutover U221, reshaped by U221b, U221c and U221d, its comparison with origin/main removed by U393) is the judge of the move, not its hand: run by the coordinator or the operator from a terminal outside every seat, in the main checkout (a linked worktree is refused), with no allow rule and no seat policy row, it is read-only. It writes nothing anywhere but its scratch directory under `$TMPDIR`, changes no mode, reads no origin, and runs no git at all but the checkout's `rev-parse` and `status`. Three rounds had the lever copy the live store itself under a control meant to catch a writer during the run (two observations of the source held equal, then the store made unwritable by mode and a staging directory renamed into place under an identity hold), and each round's audit found a same-user writer the control did not see (verdicts U221-e82581f3, U221-738e89ca and U221-595a116a, class move-race); the mechanism change of decisions 2026-11-08T10:26:46Z (form remove) takes the copy phase out of the lever, because a same-user writer defeats every observation, mode and identity control, and the one actor who can make the host quiescent is the operator. Before the move (the derived store `${REGIME_STORE_ROOT}/<program>` absent; the store root must exist, as `hcom-seat` makes it, U219) the lever walks the live store with the derived walk U220 gave `records-sync.sh` (`find ! -type d` through `read_listing`; every entry a regular file below the store with no symlink component, no control character, no `..` and no name beginning with `.`, refused by name otherwise; `redact-words.txt` included, since the new root is the live store), counts its files and prints the verdict `ready: <n> live files; the derived store is absent` and the operator's procedure, seven numbered lines, each a complete command or one sentence. The live store may be ahead of origin/main, and is after every records sync: the sync's listing is taken when `records-sync.sh` builds its commit, and before that pull request can merge the coordinator writes its verdict row and file, its sweep row and set, and after the merge its merge row (measured 2026-11-08 after sync 52, PR 738: nine paths main lacked or differed in the moment the sync landed). U221's gate, which refused the move until the live store masked equalled main at the published prefix, could therefore never pass and guarded nothing the procedure does not check itself, so U393 removed it (form remove): line 3 proves the copy, line 7 proves the checkout store clean against HEAD, and the next sync, listed from the derived store (U220), publishes whatever the live store held beyond main.

```text
1. Stop every seat and end every coordinator process: run regime-stop --all in <checkout> (the stop of the kit, regime-start --stop --all), confirm with hcom list -v that no seat of this checkout is alive, and close every session that runs a lever of the kit against it.
2. cp -a <checkout>/.orchestration/orchestrate/<program> <root>/<program> && chmod -R go-rwx <root>/<program>
3. diff -r <checkout>/.orchestration/orchestrate/<program> <root>/<program> && find <root>/<program> -type f | wc -l   # expect no line from diff and the count <n>
4. git -C <checkout> checkout -- .orchestration/orchestrate/<program>
5. git -C <checkout> clean -fd -- .orchestration/orchestrate/<program>
6. git -C <checkout> pull --ff-only
7. regime-store-move <program>
```

The procedure is printed, never run: it is the operator's, to be run by hand once the last records sync from inside has merged, on a host made quiescent by line 1 (the operator is the one actor who can stop every seat and end every coordinator process, which no lever can observe its way around); line 2 is the copy, with the modes and times kept and then the group and other bits removed below the copy in the same line (`cp -a` alone keeps the checkout store's 0755, and line 7 holds the program store to 0700, the mode the kit gives every store it makes; U221e), line 3 its proof, and lines 4 to 6 are the last write to the coordinator's live checkout by anyone, the lever or a procedure included (standing order 40, struck, decisions 2026-11-05T23:34:51Z: the class of writes to the live main checkout ends with it, because after the step the store is no longer in the working tree and `pull --ff-only` moves a clean checkout). After the move (the derived store present, a 0700 directory of this user; a link, a file or another mode there refuses by name, and a directory with no file, regime-init's empty one, refuses too) line 7 reports the moved state: the derived store's files are counted and `git status --porcelain` of the checkout's store subtree is read (under `--no-optional-locks`, so the read refreshes no index); empty, the lever prints `moved: the derived store holds <m> files; the checkout store is clean against HEAD` and exits 0, else `refused: the checkout store is not clean (<k> entries); finish the step`. After that every lever's `store: derived` form is the one in use, and the pre-move forms are removed by a later unit. Every producer's status is read and its stderr captured (U221e: every read runs through `read_listing`, every listing is NUL-delimited and read with `IFS= read -r -d ''`, so a name with a trailing space reaches the shape check and is refused by name): a walk, a count, the mode, the toplevel or `git status` that fails or warns refuses with exit 2 naming the producer; a write under the scratch directory that fails exits 1 naming it; the scratch directory, which holds every write of the run (the producers' outputs and the live store's file list), is removed on every exit with the removal's status read, and a removal that fails ends a `ready` or `moved` run with exit 1 naming the directory.

## `read-lib.sh` and `producer-status-check.sh`: every producer read has three outcomes

Regime-cutover U112 (plan P-21, standing order 21). `read-lib.sh` is a helper the five GitHub-write levers (`records-sync.sh`, `pr-audit.sh`, `pr-merge.sh`, `pr-threads-check.sh`, `pr-body-edit.sh`) source from the directory they run from (`LEVER_DIR`), pinned and self-checked like `regime-env`, never run, so it has no allow rule. It defines three readers. Each returns 0 with the value in the named variable (or the output file), 1 for an absence (the variable empty), and 2 for a failed read with one line on stderr naming the key, the file or the producer and the status. No reader exits: the lever maps 1 and 2 itself, and a 2 stops it before any write.

| Reader                                                      | 0                                                                           | 1                                                                       | 2                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `read_config <git function> <key> <variable> <stderr file>` | `git config --get` exited 0 with a non-empty value and an empty stderr file | exit 1 with an empty stderr file (unset), or exit 0 with an empty value | any other status (128 measured for an unparsable file on git 2.56.0; 3 is never special-cased), or anything in the stderr file whatever the status (an unreadable `-f` file exits 1 with only a warning)  |
| `read_listing <output file> <stderr file> <producer...>`    | exit 0, an empty stderr file, a non-empty output file                       | exit 0, an empty stderr file, an empty output file                      | a nonzero status (127 when the producer cannot be run), or anything in the stderr file (`git ls-files` warns and exits 0 for a directory it cannot open; `find` prints what it reached before it exits 1) |
| `read_file <path> <variable>`                               | a non-empty file, its trailing newlines kept                                | an empty file                                                           | no file at the path, or `cat` exited nonzero (unreadable, a directory, cut short)                                                                                                                         |

`scripts/ci/producer-status-check.sh` holds the levers to the grammar those readers satisfy: a walk over `shfmt -ln bash --to-json`, bound to `scripts/ci/shfmt_json_contract.py` like `masked-dirname-check.sh`, whose findings are `file:line: <rule>: <message>`.

- a. `substitution`: a `$(…)` or backquoted substitution is accepted only as the whole value of the one assignment of a statement that is the last of an `if` or `while` condition or the left side of a `||` whose branch handles the status, when no statement on the path from that statement to the top level (the statement itself, the `||` list, and every block, subshell, list, `if`, loop or function body around it) runs in the background, is negated (the condition's own `!` aside) or is a `coproc`, and no process substitution (`<(…)`, `>(…)`) is on that path (`! x=$(…) || exit 1` reads the failure as success, `x=$(…) || exit 1 &`, `{ x=$(…) || exit 1; } &` and `( x=$(…) || exit 1 ) &` end only the background job, `coproc { x=$(…); } || exit 1` tests the coprocess's start, `cat <(x=$(…) || exit 1)` and `tee >(x=$(…) || exit 1)` run the read in a child whose status the parent never sees, measured, bash goes on with exit 0: each is a finding naming the flagged statement's or the substitution's line; a function body is not the top level, so `f() { x=$(…) || return 1; }` is judged by its own path, whatever `f &` does at the call). The branches that handle it are a closed list: a plain command whose known first word is `exit` or `return`, as the whole branch, with no argument (the failed status propagates), with one argument whose known text is a decimal from 1 to 255, or with one argument that is the bare `$?` (`exit 0`, `return 0`, `exit "$x"`, `exit $(cmd)` and `exit 1 2` are findings: a zero turns the failed read into success); a plain command whose known first word names a function the file defines exactly once (every definition counts, at any depth: a second one inside an `if`, a loop, a block or another function, or a second spelling, is a finding naming both lines, since bash runs whichever ran last), at top level, in the foreground and not negated (`true() { exit 2; } &` defines it in a child shell only), before the tested statement in file order, by a name of the closed allowlist `refuse`, `fail` (the two refusal functions the levers define; a denylist of bash builtins and reserved words cannot be complete, and `fc` was the one the lint's tuple missed: bash runs the builtin when the function is absent, so a call of any other defined name is a finding saying it is not a refusal name, whatever its body), whose body is the refusal grammar, matched and never analysed: zero or more statements, each a plain command whose known first word is `echo` or `printf` with a redirect that sends fd 1 to fd 2 (`>&2` or `1>&2`), then exactly one last statement `exit` with one argument whose known text is a decimal from 1 to 255, every statement in the foreground and not negated, and nothing else of any kind (no `return`, no `$?`, no `local`, no `if`, no assignment, no second `exit`, no call of another function, no command substitution anywhere in the body: `f() { echo failed >&2; return $?; }`, `f() { return 0; exit 1; }`, `f() { echo x; exit 2; }` and `f() { local m=$1; echo "$m" >&2; exit 2; }` are each a finding naming the function and the first statement outside the grammar, by line; a name no top-level function defines is not accepted; a function that cleans up before it exits is not a refusal function, so the cleanup goes in a trap or the call carries an allow comment naming the cleanup line); a block or subshell whose statements are that grammar (`{ echo no >&2; exit 1; }`), with `return <1-255>` as the one terminator instead when the branch is a brace block and the `||` list sits inside a function body of the file (U216b: a sourced helper returns where a lever exits, `regime-env`'s `|| { echo … >&2; return 1; }` blocks; a `return` at the top level of a script is a bash error, so it ends nothing there and stays a finding; U216c: a subshell's `return` ends the subshell alone while the function goes on, measured, so a subshell branch ending in `return` is a finding naming the subshell wherever it sits; a bare `exit` or `return`, a `return` outside every function body or inside a subshell, a call of a refusal function or a `$?` inside one is a finding); a bare `=` assignment of exactly `$?` (`status+=$?` adds the status to the value, 25 and 6 making 256, and is a finding naming the append). A branch statement run in the background (`exit 2 &`) or negated (`! exit 1`, `! (exit 1)`) handles nothing. Any other branch (a pipeline, an `&&` or `||` list, an `if`, a `case`, `true`, `:`, `false` or any other plain command, a block or subshell outside the grammar, an assignment of anything else, a first word with no known text) is a finding naming the shape; a read whose empty value a later line handles carries an allow comment naming that line (rule e). A word has a known text only when every part is a bare literal over the closed character set (letters, digits and `_ - . / : = + @ % ,`), a plain single-quoted string or double quotes around literals and such strings; `* ? [ ] { } ~ $`, a backquote, a backslash, whitespace or any other character in a bare literal, an expansion, an ANSI-C `$'…'` or a locale `$"…"` string make the word unknown, and nothing is decoded. Every other position is a finding at the line where the substitution opens. A heredoc body whose delimiter is quoted or escaped is literal to every scan of the lint, which has one traversal: a function definition or an allow comment inside such a body defines and exempts nothing.
- b. `producer`: a command whose leading words (known texts, as in a) match a row of `scripts/ci/status-blind-producers.tsv` (`git ls-files`, `git config -f`, `git config --file`, `find`, `ls`, each with its measured behaviour and research section) must capture its stderr to a file that a later `[[ ! -s <file> ]]` of the same statement list tests in a shape whose failure stops the script: the last statement of an `if` condition, or the left side of a `||` whose branch is one of the forms of rule a, judged in the same function context as a tested read's branch (U216c: inside a function body a brace block ending in `return <1-255>` closes the producer, a subshell ending in `return` does not); a bare `[[ ! -s <file> ]]` statement counts only at the top level of a file whose prologue (rule c) runs `set -euo pipefail` before it, where `set -e` ends the script on its failure, and anywhere else (a function body, a block, a file with no prologue before it) it is not a test. The producer, the test and every statement on the path of each to the top level must be in the foreground and not negated, with no process substitution on the path (`git ls-files > l 2> e &` lets the next statement test `e` before the listing has finished, `! git ls-files > l 2> e` reads its failure as success, `{ git ls-files > l 2> e; } &` ends only the job and `coproc git ls-files > l 2> e` tests the coprocess's start and `done < <(git ls-files)` runs it in a child whose failure the parent never sees, so each is a finding naming the flag and its line whatever follows it; `! [[ ! -s err ]] || exit 1` reads a non-empty file as clean, `[[ ! -s err ]] &` ends only the job), and the leftmost statement of an `&&` list or a pipeline is not a test; a producer whose only such statement is one of those is a finding naming that statement's line and why. Stderr is captured only when the last redirect that touches fd 2 is `2>`, `2>>` or `2>|` to a word whose known text is not `/dev/null` or whose parts are literals and parameter expansions (a process substitution, `2> >(tee e)`, is neither, and one among a producer's words, `ls <(…)`, has no known text); the descriptor written before an operator is read as bash reads it, a decimal (`02>&1` and `002> err` name fd 2; one that is not all digits, `{fd}>`, refuses the file, exit 2), and a redirect touches fd 2 when that descriptor is 2 whatever the operator, when it is `&>` or `&>>`, or when it is `>&` or `<&` to a word that is not a plain descriptor (all digits or `-`), whatever the descriptor before it (`>&file` and `1>&file` send both streams to the file, `>&2-`, `1>&2-` and `<&2-` move fd 2 and close it, `>&$fd` may be any of them when it runs; `>&2`, `1>&2`, `01>&2` and `>&-` leave fd 2 alone); any other operator there leaves it uncaptured, and the word of a `>&` or `<&` is never the captured file. The tested word is the same file only when both texts are known and equal, or both hold a parameter expansion and their parts are equal. `read_listing` is that shape by construction, and a producer the table does not name is not held to the rule.
- c. `prologue`: `set -euo pipefail` runs at top level, in the foreground and not negated, before the first statement holding a substitution, else a finding at line 1 naming why the first one the file holds does not count (`set -euo pipefail &`, `{ set -euo pipefail; } &` and `( set -euo pipefail )` set the options of a child shell only; one inside a function, block or `if` is not at the top level).
- d. `read`: a `read` that carries its own `<` redirect is a finding naming `read_file`.
- e. `allow`: `# producer-status: allow <reason>` on the statement's first line exempts it from a, b and d; without a reason the comment is itself a finding.
- f. `shadow` and `binding`: the rules trust a closed set of names by their spelling, `GUARDED_NAMES` in the lint, one dict with a reason per name: the builtins the rules read (`set`, c; `exit`, `return`, a; `echo`, `printf`, the refusal grammar; `read`, d; `declare`, `typeset`, `readonly`, `export`, `local`, whose words this rule reads), the refusal functions (`refuse`, `fail`), the lint's own rebinders (`alias`, `unalias`, `shopt`, `enable`, `hash`, `unset`, `builtin`, `command`, `eval`, `source`, `.`, `trap`, `mapfile`, `readarray`; the set is closed over itself), the library's arrays (`GIT_ISOLATED_ENV`, `GIT_ISOLATED_ARGS`, `GIT_ISOLATED_SCRUB_NAMES`), the directory the one accepted `source` reads from (`LEVER_DIR`), the variables that bind a command word (`PATH`, `BASH_ALIASES`, `BASH_CMDS`) and the ones bash itself reads when it runs a lever (`BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, `IFS`, `CDPATH`, `BASH_LOADABLES_PATH`, `EXECIGNORE`, `PS4`); a test holds every literal name the lint compares against to that set, so it cannot drift. A write is any Assign node whose name is guarded, with or without a value (`PATH=`, `export PATH`, `IFS= read -r x`, `declare -a GIT_ISOLATED_ENV=(x)`), the variable of a `for` or `select`, an expansion that assigns its parameter (`${PATH:=x}`), a word in any position whose leading literal parts spell an assignment to a guarded name (`export 'PATH=/tmp/x:$PATH'`, `env PATH=/tmp/x git ls-files`, `'GIT_ISOLATED_ENV=PATH=/tmp/x'`, `alias fail=true`) or any of whose literal parts holds the name of a guarded variable as a token (every guarded name the shadow reading does not take as a command word: `read -r PATH`, `printf -v PATH x`, `unset PATH`, `local -n r=PATH`, `(( PATH = 1 ))`, `let 'PATH = 1'`, `bash -c 'cd /; PATH=x git'`, a message naming it; the name is matched in the concatenation of the word's literal parts, quotes removed and in order, so `printf -v PA"TH" x`, `PA'TH'=1` and `'PA'TH=1` are findings, and an expansion among the parts contributes no text and hides none), a word of a declaration (directly or through `builtin`/`command`) with no known text (`export "$binding"`, `export "${GIT_ISOLATED_ENV[@]}"`), a declaration option under which a later assignment writes elsewhere (`declare -i`, `local -n`), and an operand where bash evaluates arithmetic (`(( ))`, `$(( ))`, `let`, a C-style `for`, an array subscript, a slice, `-eq` and the other numeric tests, `-v`) that is not digits, operators and whitespace alone: bash evaluates a name there recursively, so `y='PATH=1'` then `(( y, 1 ))`, `(( $y, 1 ))`, `[[ y -eq 1 ]]`, `a[y]=1` or `let 'y + 1'` sets PATH (measured on bash 3.2), and such a statement is a finding unless its exact text, in its file, is a row of `ACCEPTED_ARITHMETIC` (the 44 statements of the adopted set measured in round 20: the `$#` tests, the status and counter comparisons and increments, `exit $((128 + $2))`, the fetch retry `for` of pr-audit.sh pinned whole; `$#`, `$?` and `${#name}` are not read as numbers, a row accepts them). The walk visits every node of every statement and asks those questions of each; there is no list of positions. A write is accepted only when the exact text of its statement, in its file, is a row of `ACCEPTED_WRITES`, the writes the adopted set makes (measured on the lint, the three libraries, `regime-env` since U216b, and the five levers: 26 rows, the `IFS=` prefixes of the levers' `read` lines, `LEVER_DIR=${dir}` of every self-check, the library's three arrays, their `declare`, the `unset` loop over their names, `export "${GIT_ISOLATED_ENV[@]}"`, five messages that name a guarded variable); anything else is a `binding` finding naming the name, how it is written, the statement's kind and the line. The command words keep the `shadow` reading: a function definition of any name the rules read as one (`set() { :; }`, `eval() { :; }`, `export() { :; }`), at any depth and in any spelling, and a plain command whose known first word is a rebinder, read as bash reads it (`alias` or `unalias` in any form; `shopt` naming `expand_aliases` or with an argument of no known text; `enable`, `hash` or `unset` naming such a word or with an argument of no known text, `unset -v` aside; `builtin` or `command` naming one or with an argument of no known text, its command judged by its own word; `eval` with any argument; `source` or `.` with anything but `. "${LEVER_DIR}/git-isolated.sh"`, `. "${LEVER_DIR}/read-lib.sh"` or `. "${LEVER_DIR}/regime-env"`, the three libraries the levers source (U216b); `trap` with an action that is not empty, `-` or one simple command whose first word is no rebinder; `mapfile` or `readarray` with `-C`; the options read per word, `source`, `.` and `eval` skipping none), are each a finding naming what is shadowed. Not read: an expansion handed to an external command (`env "$x" git`: that command's argument, which the shell does not bind; the array the library hands `env` is pinned by its row), a name an expansion carries into a builtin that is no declaration (`unset -v "$name"` of the scrubs, `printf -v "$3"` of the readers), and a literal heredoc body. No allow comment exempts rule f.

The enforced file set is a test, not a Makefile target: `tests/unit/test_producer_status_check.py` runs the lint over `read-lib.sh`, `git-isolated.sh` and the five levers and asserts exit 0 with no output, and over the three libraries and the five levers together (`regime-env` is the third library since U216b, sourced by `pr-merge.sh`) asserting that the only findings are the four the helper has of its own, pinned by line and rule: no `set -euo pipefail` at its top level (the two other libraries carry one), and three `|| name=''` branches of its self-check without the allow comment `read-lib.sh`'s same lines carry; a unit that may edit the helper clears them and moves it into the zero-findings assertion. `tests/unit/test_read_lib.py` holds each reader to a failure-injection test by name.

## `claude-keyed`: the bridge's Claude Code child runs on an API key read from one file

Regime-cutover U510 (`handoff/47-api-key-route-design.md`; handoff/43 and handoff/46 for the cause). A seat's model turn ran on the seats' shared claude.ai login under `<seats-root>/.state/claude`, and the OAuth server's refresh-token rotation revoked that login five times between 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-13, each time after a turn made from a copy of its credentials (standing order 54). The route that ends the class is a credential with no refresh token: one API key from the operator's Enterprise organization's Claude Console, handed to the Claude Code child alone. `scripts/orchestrator/claude-keyed` is that hand-off, installed and pinned like every lever above (`claude.levers.sha256.claude-keyed`) and run by nobody but the Agent SDK: the pi-claude-bridge spawns `provider.pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` where it spawned Claude Code (`config.ts:61`, `:110`; patch 06 made the per-profile `~/.omp/profiles/<role>/agent/claude-bridge.json` the one source), so the lever `exec`s the one Claude Code binary of the bridge's own SDK package, `<bridge>/node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-<platform>/claude` (`<bridge>` is `omp.extension_providers.claude-bridge` of the manifest beside the lever, required to be a row of `claude.levers.extensions`; `<platform>` comes from a closed table over `uname -s` and `uname -m`, Darwin arm64 `darwin-arm64`, Darwin x86_64 `darwin-x64`, Linux x86_64 `linux-x64`, Linux aarch64 `linux-arm64`, any other pair refused naming both words, and on Linux `-musl` is appended when and only when a file matches the musl loader glob `/lib/ld-musl-*.so*`, which `CLAUDE_KEYED_MUSL_MARKER_GLOB` renames for the tests; the SDK installs the glibc and the musl package of one architecture side by side, so U510c replaced the glob count that refused on two matches, and the one path must be an executable regular file and no link, else one refusal naming it), with its own arguments unchanged, stdin and stdout untouched, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` exported from the key file and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` unset (the bridge README: an exported gateway variable redirects the child). Nothing else of the environment changes; the key is never an argument, a file the lever writes, a log line or a message. Every name the lever assigns is unset before anything is assigned (U510e, verdict U510-88ae09c6): an exported variable of one of those names keeps its export attribute through an assignment (a `local` of the same name inherits it), so without the unset the key, the mode or a path would reach a producer's or the child's environment under a second name; those names leave the child's environment and nothing else of it changes. The manifest is the one the lever's own physical directory names (U510d): `<root>/.agents/agent-config.yaml` for the installed copy `<root>/.local/bin/common/claude-keyed`, whose `~/` spellings expand through that root, the checkout's `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml` for the copy under `scripts/orchestrator`, and a copy in neither layout refuses; a caller's HOME steers neither the pin nor the bridge directory the key reaches. `#!/bin/bash -p` is pr-audit.sh's and router-gh's form (U465f): a poisoned environment replaces no builtin the lever runs, and the self pin check runs before anything else is read. `bash -p` keeps PATH, and inside a seat PATH holds the seat's tool directory and the caller's, so the lever's own producers (`uname`, `stat`, `cat`, `wc`, `awk`) run on `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` alone, hcom-seat's form, and the caller's PATH is the child's again on the exec line (U510d); the self-check's `sed` and hash tool keep the kit's shared form, which `tests/unit/test_lever_self_check.py` holds every shell lever but hcom-seat to.

The key file is the one path `REGIME_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE` names (U512 makes hcom-seat set it to `<seats-root>/.state/anthropic/api-key` for every role; until then a launch without it refuses, never falls back to the login): an absolute path to a regular file of this user, mode 0600, no symbolic link in its last component (`-L` on the spelled path and on its physical directory's form), holding one line of the closed class `^sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}$` ending in one line feed or none. A second line, a carriage return, a trailing space, a NUL or any other byte refuses naming the file, never the content. The directory that holds the file is held too (U510d): its spelled last component no link, this user's, mode 0700, else one refusal naming the directory and the file, since the checks on the file and its read each open the path anew and a writer of a looser directory could swap a link in between. Every producer's status is read (standing order 21): one `stat` call each for the directory's and the file's mode and owner (compared with the shell's EUID, so a planted `stat` that names another owner refuses), `cat` for the bytes with `wc -c` against what the shell holds (a NUL bash drops is seen), `uname`, and `awk` over the manifest by indentation as `seat-login-preflight` reads its overlays. Each refusal is one stderr line `claude-keyed: refused: <reason>`, exit 2, nothing started; a line boundary in a path the environment named is folded to a space first, and where bash itself opens the file (the `wc -c <` redirection) the block's stderr is dropped before the open, so a path holding a newline or the file gone between `cat` and `wc` still yields one line (U510e). `--check` runs every check and prints `key file ok: <path>; executable: <path>` (exit 0): the preflight and the doctor call it (U512, U513), and the operator's handout ends with it. `--dry-run [<argument>...]` prints the command it would exec with the key as `<redacted>` and the names it unsets.

The bridge reaches the lever through a generated chezmoi modify script per role, `home/dot_omp/private_profiles/private_<role>/private_agent/modify_claude-bridge.json`, deployed on `~/.omp/profiles/<role>/agent/claude-bridge.json` for every role whose bridge child runs: a seat role whose omp model route is `claude-bridge` (orchestrator, worker) and every gateway role (dsh, whose `omp auth-gateway stdio` child serves `claude-bridge/<model>` through the same extension); the audit role, on openai-siwc, gets none. The script is `modify_private_config.yml`'s form (`#!/bin/sh`, `set -eu`, the standard library's json alone) on the system `python3 -I`, the kit's baseline, which the apply container has (U510b; the first form ran under uv, which that container lacks, and its silent passthrough left the three files unwritten and the login route in place): it sets `provider.pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` to `~/.local/bin/common/claude-keyed` (the applying user's home, so the file is no template), keeps every other key in its order and writes `json.dumps(indent=2)` and a newline. Its contract (U510e, verdict U510-88ae09c6) is idempotence of its own output, a second run on what it wrote changes nothing, which is what the output index's modify comparison measures (`check-agent-runtime.py` runs the script on the live file and compares the bytes), and the provider row's presence, which the doctor's claude-keyed row checks by key (U513); it is not byte equality with the bridge's own `JSON.stringify(next, null, 2)` rewrite (`config.ts:29`, `{...existing}`, the `startupNoticeShown` record), whose float form differs (JSON.stringify writes 1e-7 where json.dumps writes 1e-07), so a bridge rewrite that carries a float reads as a change once, until the next apply writes the script's form again; without python3 it prints one stderr line naming the file and exits 1 with nothing on stdout, so `chezmoi apply` fails naming the file (fail closed, never the login route by silence). The live file sits in the seat's own write root, so its content is the seat's (U510d): on an empty, non-UTF-8, unparseable or non-object input, a NaN or an Infinity (refused at the parse by `parse_constant` and never written, `allow_nan=False`; `json.loads` admitted them and `json.dumps` re-emitted them, so the file became non-JSON, the bridge's `tryParseJson` read `{}` and the SDK fell back to its bundled binary on the login, silently), a lone surrogate escape or nesting past the recursion limit, the script prints the managed object alone with one warning naming the file (one line per run: when more than one condition applies, the first met names the reason, U510e), exit 0, so `chezmoi apply` never clobbers the bridge's file, never writes one the bridge cannot parse and never fails on the file's content. The directories carry chezmoi's `private_` attribute (the kit's earlier spelling of this root) so an apply keeps the 0700 modes hcom-seat gives the profile directories it makes. The output index deploys each as a `modify` entry; the root has no `GENERATED_SCOPES` row, since the deployed glob would read the bridge's own `claude-bridge.json` of a role on another route as an orphan, and the generator's rule-root check refuses a modify script no role renders by name instead.

Measured 2026-11-13 with a fake key through the real SDK binary (brief step 4, no login): `REGIME_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE=<fake 0600 file> CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<scratch> claude-keyed -p --output-format json 'Reply with the single word pong.'` answers the API's `Invalid API key · Fix external API key`, so the key reached the API and nothing read the login; `--check` on the same file prints the ok line and on a 0644 copy refuses with its mode.

| Control                                                                   | Disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `scripts/orchestrator/claude-keyed`                                       | closed: `tests.unit.test_claude_keyed` (the real bash against a fixture bridge whose fake SDK binary records argv, PATH and variable names: the exec path with the key present, the three names unset and the caller's PATH back, `--check` and `--dry-run` byte for byte, every refusal by name, the key file's directory held, each producer failed through the one-line test variant and never consulted on the caller's PATH by the pristine copy, every read injected (the directory's stat and the file's, both manifest reads, cat, wc, uname, sed and the hash tool, and the `cd` and `pwd -P` of the directory hold through a shim function in the variant), every name the lever assigns unset first (an exported `key`, `key_text`, `mode` or `LEVER_MANIFEST` reaches neither a producer nor the child, and the unset list equals the names the text assigns), one stderr line for a key path holding a newline and for the file gone between cat and wc, the installed copy's manifest chosen by its own root under any HOME, the altered copy refused by the pin, and the fixture key's bytes absent from every output) |
| the key's reach                                                           | closed in the lever: the child's environment alone; residual (owner U514): the seat's bash tool shares the sandbox and the uid, so it can read the key file and the exec'd child's environment (`ps eww`, `/proc/<pid>/environ`, a core dump of the child), the class the Keychain login already had (U190); the Console revokes a key and bounds a workspace's spend, and a key-holding proxy outside the sandbox, which U514 designs against both reads, is the closure                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `home/dot_omp/private_profiles/*/private_agent/modify_claude-bridge.json` | closed: `tests.unit.test_generate_agent_configs` (rendered for the bridge-routed roles only, byte for byte; the merge keeps `startupNoticeShown` and the key order and is idempotent on its own output, a float surviving two runs in value, and claims no byte equality with the bridge's JSON.stringify rewrite; empty and invalid input, a NaN, an Infinity, a lone surrogate, deep nesting, a byte order mark and non-UTF-8 bytes each yield the managed object alone, strict JSON, with one warning, one line when two conditions apply); `make render-check` holds the files and the index to the manifest                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| the login as a fallback                                                   | closed: every refusal exits 2 with nothing started; no path of the lever runs Claude Code without the key, and the login names are unset on the one path that runs it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
